TI From social entrepreneurial education: how self-efficacy and family
   support shape youth entrepreneurial intentions
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Social entrepreneurial education; youth entrepreneurial intention;
   self-efficacy; family support; theory of planned behavior; Arts &
   Humanities; Humanities; Media & Film Studies; Journalism & Professional
   Media; Publishing; Arts; Music; Music & Education; Early Years; Social
   Sciences; Education
ID DETERMINANTS; IMPACT; INSTRUMENT; STUDENTS
AB This study investigated the impact of self-efficacy (SE) and family support (FS) on social entrepreneurial education (SEE) and youth entrepreneurial intention (YEI) in Ghana. Grounded in the Theory of Planned Behavior (TPB) and Social Cognitive Theory (SCT), it explores the mechanisms through which these factors shape entrepreneurial aspirations. A structured questionnaire was distributed to 423 youths undergoing training with the Ghana Enterprise Agency, and data analysis was conducted using SmartPLS-SEM (4) to test the direct, mediating, and moderating effects. The findings indicate that SEE has a significant impact on YEI, with SE fully mediating the relationship between SEE and YEI and FS moderating this relationship. These results highlight social entrepreneurship (SE) and financial sustainability (FS) as crucial factors in fostering entrepreneurial intention through social entrepreneurial education. This study highlights the importance of policymakers and educators integrating SEE into formal and informal educational systems to foster entrepreneurial mind-sets and drive societal change. By incorporating the TPB framework and SCT, this study provides novel insights into how SE and FS enhance the effectiveness of SEE in shaping youth entrepreneurial intentions and offers practical implications for fostering entrepreneurship in emerging economies.
C1 [Oduro Owusu, Samuel; Thomas Gil, Mathew] Manipal Acad Higher Educ, Manipal Sch Commerce & Econ, Manipal 576104, Karnataka, India.

TI William James's pragmatism in education, from experiential learning to
   global application
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Experiential learning; individualized instruction; pedagogical
   innovation; William James; Vietnam's educational policy; Social
   Sciences; Education; Criminology and Criminal Justice; Development
   Studies, Environment, Social Work, Urban Studies
AB The strength of William James's pragmatic educational philosophy lies in his emphasis on integrating practical elements into the learning process. This philosophy allows learners to have direct experiences and personalizes education for each individual. This is considered a core principle in James's pragmatic pedagogical method. He viewed education as an essential means for individuals to interact with reality and adapt to real-world conditions. From there, learners use knowledge to proactively solve problems in their lives. This perspective is reflected through educational goals, teaching methods, and learner participation as a process of discovery and development in the learner's transformation. Our research will compare James's theoretical framework with that of other philosophers, from experiential learning theory to digital transformation in education to support the development of critical thinking, creativity, and self-direction in learners. Next, this article discusses the significance of James's ideas in modern educational reform, particularly in Vietnam's transition period, where Vietnamese education is geared towards practical, competency-based learning. Education also needs to promote flexible teaching methods that are learner-centered and practice-oriented. Thus, James's educational philosophy has provided us with profound insights into current global educational innovation.
C1 [Kien, Pham Thi; Dung, Bui Xuan] Univ Econ Ho Chi Minh City UEH, Philosophy, Ho Chi Minh City, Vietnam.

TI AI-assisted learning: ChatGPT for anamnesis in women's health education
SO MEDICAL EDUCATION ONLINE
LA English
DT Article
DE ChatGPT; large language models; woman health; anamnesis; education
ID ARTIFICIAL-INTELLIGENCE; DOCUMENTATION; INFORMATION; PATIENT
AB Artificial intelligence (AI) tools such as ChatGPT are rapidly being adopted in medical education, yet evidence regarding their impact on learners' history-taking (anamnesis) skills remains limited. This gap is particularly relevant in women's health education, where high-quality anamnesis is essential for addressing sensitive, context-dependent concerns and for ensuring the safety of two interrelated patients: mother and fetus. Methods: This study comprised two phases. First, semi-structured virtual interviews were administered to students and faculty to assess their familiarity with ChatGPT and access to digital technologies. Second, an in-person classroom intervention was implemented within the Women's Health discipline, integrating ChatGPT into history-taking instruction. Students' anamnesis performance was evaluated before and after ChatGPT-assisted learning. Additionally, a novel instructional activity was introduced in which ChatGPT generated cases classified as plausible, intermediate, or physiologically impossible, serving as prompts for guided discussion focused on diagnostic pitfalls, critical appraisal, and clinical reasoning. Results: In the virtual phase, faculty (n = 62) and students (n = 115) completed the questionnaires. The classroom intervention involved 40 medical students participating as part of routine Women's Health teaching activities, with faculty oversight to ensure pedagogical alignment and responsible AI use. A pronounced discrepancy emerged between students' familiarity with ChatGPT (99.1%) and lower levels of familiarity among faculty. Anamnesis performance improved significantly after ChatGPT-assisted learning (p < 0.0001). Students reported increased confidence and perceived enhancement of history-taking quality, and all participants viewed ChatGPT as a valuable educational resource. The AI-generated cases effectively stimulated discussion and reinforced clinical reasoning processes. Findings contributed to the development of a practical guideline for the responsible integration of ChatGPT into Women's Health education. Conclusions: ChatGPT can enhance anamnesis and promote critical reasoning through structured interaction with AI-generated cases. However, its effective and responsible use depends on targeted faculty development to ensure alignment with pedagogical goals, appropriate oversight, and consistent integration into curriculum.

TI An empirical study on improving classroom teaching effectiveness based
   on the purposefulness, consciousness, and initiative of
   learning-evidence from China
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Classroom teaching effectiveness (CTE); College students' personal
   learning quality (CSPLQ); Statistical analysis methods; Classroom
   teaching
ID GOALS
AB At present, the reform and innovation of higher education in China focus on teachers playing a leading role in education and teaching. In implementation, classroom teaching should be student-centred. The main purpose of this article is to explore the impact of college students' personal learning quality (i.e. learning purposiveness, learning consciousness, and learning initiative (LPCI))on classroom teaching effectiveness (CTE). Focusing on the perspectives of undergraduate students and teachers, the research employs multiple statistical analysis methods to test the impact. Research data were collected through the same questionnaire at the beginning and end of the course to evaluate the impact of LPCI on teaching activities. The research conclusions are that the learning quality of college students is reflected in their purposiveness, consciousness, and initiative in learning, and teachers should focus on cultivating college students' personal learning quality (CSPLQ) in the classroom and fully consider the situations of different students' learning qualities. To improve classroom teaching effectiveness, we should fully consider the students as the main body of teaching, rather than relying only on the one - way activities such as the reform of teachers' teaching methods and the improvement of teaching levels. The viewpoint of CSPLQ proposed in this study has reference value for the research of education and teaching.

TI Student performance as a catalyst for teacher identity formation:
   evidence from Tanzania
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Teacher identity; student performance; teaching profession; secondary
   education; Teacher identity; student performance; teaching profession;
   secondary education
ID SOCIAL COGNITIVE THEORY; SELF-EFFICACY
AB Despite the government's initiatives to enact and implement the secondary education development policy, student performance trends in Tanzania remained challenging. However, the influence of student academic success on teaching professionalism is inadequately explored. This study employed a qualitative research approach to gain insights into how student performance shapes teacher identity, particularly during the implementation of the expansion policy. The research involved teachers and school principals who were purposively selected from four secondary schools in one of the regions located in the Southern Highlands of the country. Individual and focus group (FG) interviews were used to generate evidence about the study phenomenon. It was revealed that poor student performance had far-reaching negative implications for teachers' professional image, perceived competence, and overall psychological well-being. Therefore, developing and implementing practices that promote student performance can make teaching a more appealing and respected profession.

TI Modernisation of the grammar translation method: a convergence of AI
   technologies and foreign language learning
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Artificial intelligence; language teaching; grammar translation method;
   interdisciplinary learning; cognitive approach; Arts & Humanities; Arts;
   Media Communication; Humanities; Cultural Studies; Media & Film Studies;
   Media & Communications; Communication Studies
AB Artificial intelligence (AI) has transformed nearly every aspect of modern life, including education. The integration of AI tools into the classroom represents an attempt to redesign traditional, human-centred teaching methods. Educators and researchers are now investigating how digital tools, particularly AI, influence pedagogical roles, teaching strategies, and learner engagement. This article posits the efficiency of the grammar-translation method (GTM) in modern classrooms. We argue that the GTM may be meaningfully enhanced through the integration of AI teaching tools, as it customizes instruction, ameliorates error correction, and supports interdisciplinary learning.

TI Exploring social distance of iranian students from international
   students: a preliminary survey study
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Social interaction; bogardus social distance Scale (BSDS); academic
   major; higher education; international students; social closeness
ID INTERGROUP CONTACT; ANXIETY; STRESS
AB Although numerous studies have explored social distance in health, community, and intergroup relations, relatively few have examined how host students perceive international peers within higher education. This study investigates social distance between Iranian and international students at a major university. The target population included approximately 11,000 domestic students across faculties and academic major s, from which a stratified random sample of 378 was drawn using Cochran's formula with a 5% margin of error and 95% confidence level. Data were collected through the Bogardus Social Distance Scale (BSDS), which was cross-culturally adapted, validated, and confirmed reliable in a pilot test (alpha = 0.891). Descriptive statistics, t-tests, and ANOVA were employed to analyse variations across nationality, age, gender, and academic major. Findings indicate that Iranian students reported the highest social closeness toward Kurdish Iraqi peers (mean = 4.00) and the greatest distance toward Afghan students (mean = 2.60). Gender and age showed no significant effects, but PhD students expressed greater distance toward Kurdish and Arab Iraqi peers compared to undergraduates and Master's students. Theoretically, the study extends Social Identity and cultural distance perspectives to a non-Western context. Also, it provides a validated BSDS and a stratified sampling design, offering a framework for examining intercultural dynamics in higher education.

AF Wang, Shuoning

TI Student perceptions and attitudes toward Communicative Language Teaching
   and Intercultural Communicative Competence cultivation at Zhejiang
   Industrial Polytechnic College
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Communicative Language Teaching (CLT); Intercultural Communicative
   Competence (ICC); perceptions and attitudes toward CLT; EFL students;
   engagement in ICC activities
ID TEACHERS
AB The objective of this research is to investigate the attitudes and perceptions of students towards Communicative Language Teaching (CLT), as well as their perspectives on the cultivation of Intercultural Communicative Competence (ICC) at Zhejiang Industrial Polytechnic College. Using a quantitative cross-sectional survey, this study examined 370 freshman EFL students' perceptions of CLT and ICC through an online questionnaire distributed via social media. . Data analysis was done using SPSS, which involved descriptive statistics, Pearson's correlation, and linear regression tests. The results indicated an advantageous association between perceptions of CLT and attitudes towards the cultivation of ICC, as well as the degree of participation in ICC activities. Despite the problems that arose in implementing CLT, it increased the level of participation in pro-intercultural activities. Therefore, the students' attitudes towards CLT made a significant contribution to their intercultural learning experiences. Promoting a favourable attitude towards CLT affects students' ICC. The study's limitations include its cross-sectional design, reliance on respondent data, and limited participation to first-year EFL students. These issues limit the study's validity, generalisability, and causal implications. Future research should follow people over an extended period, employ objective methods more than self-reported data and involve a more varied population.

TI How does mathematics self-concept shape approaches to learning? Evidence
   from prospective mathematics teachers
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Mathematics self-concept; approaches to learning mathematics;
   prospective mathematics teachers; structural equation modelling; teacher
   education; Arts & Humanities; Arts; Media Communication; Humanities;
   Cultural Studies; Media & Film Studies; Media & Communications;
   Communication Studies
ID QUALITATIVE DIFFERENCES
AB We examined how mathematics self-concept influences approaches to learning mathematics among prospective mathematics teachers (PMTs) in Nigeria. Using a cross-sectional survey design within the context of students' approaches to learning theory, we generated datasets from 200 final-year PMTs across government-owned higher institutions in Nigeria. This theory provides a framework for explaining differences in approaches to learning and the factors that affect them. We measured the constructs using validated instruments and analysed the dataset through structural equation modelling. The measurement models demonstrated appropriate construct validity with omega reliability coefficients ranging from .788 to .879. Contrary to theoretical expectations, mathematics self-concept showed a significant negative effect on deep approaches to learning (beta = -.685, p < .001) and a significant positive effect on surface approaches to learning (beta = .307, p < .001), with the structural model exhibiting an appropriate fit (comparative fit index = .955, Tucker-Lewis index = .941, standardised root mean square residual = .055). These suggest that within the PMTs context, strong mathematics self-concept may be grounded in examination-oriented success, thereby reinforcing surface learning strategies rather than conceptual engagement. Conversely, lower self-concept appears to prompt compensatory deep learning efforts. These findings are theoretically provocative as they challenge the directions of effects between research constructs.

TI The evaluation of communicative and cognitive competencies among tourism
   specialists: the impact of digital tools
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Cognitive abilities; communicative competencies; digitalization of
   education; innovative technologies; tourism curriculum
ID CONTEXT
AB Digitalization in the field of education has become a significant factor influencing the development of students' cognitive and communicative skills. This study aims to assess the impact of digital technologies on the development of professional competencies among students in Kazakhstan - an issue of particular relevance given the growing importance of digital literacy and intercultural communication in the training of tourism specialists. The research problem lies in the need to examine the impact of digital tools on learning outcomes compared to traditional teaching methods. To address this, the study evaluates the effectiveness of digital tools in professional education, specifically focusing on cognitive and communication skills among students. The participant sample consisted of 100 students assigned to either the experimental or control group. The experimental group received 8-week training incorporating innovative digital technologies, while the control group was taught using traditional methods. Their scores were compared using validated cognitive and communicative tests to assess the effectiveness of the training program. The study employed an experimental approach with pre- and post-testing. The results revealed a marked improvement in both cognitive and communicative skills in the experimental group, which utilized digital technology (p < 0.001). The control group showed no statistically significant changes. The practical significance of the research lies in the fact that its findings can serve as a foundation for the development of more efficient educational programs in the field of tourism, equipping students with the necessary skills to meet the modern requirements of the global tourism industry.

TI The role of evidence-based data and analytics in medical education
   management
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Academic metrics; academic performance; analytics; digitalization;
   institutional practices; management; power BI
AB In the context of digitalization in education and increasing demands for evidence-based decision-making, the tools of educational analytics have gained particular importance. This study investigated the influence of academic data, visualized through Power BI, on managerial decisions. The unit of analysis comprised educational programs, with aggregated data on student performance metrics across three levels of training: undergraduate, internship, and residency. The sample included approximately 1,900 students from the NAO 'Astana Medical University' (Kazakhstan). A study was conducted integrating quantitative analysis of academic performance with a qualitative examination of managerial decision-making related to educational programs. Data sources included Power BI analytical dashboards, administrative documentation, and semi-structured interviews with university administrators (n = 6). The objective was to trace the relationship between academic metrics and managerial interventions. The results demonstrated positive trends: the proportion of students with unsatisfactory grades decreased from 6.76% (2024) to 1.27% (2025), while the share of high-achieving students increased from 26.14% to 29.54%. The most effective departments were those with an established culture of data-driven decision-making. Managerial measures included curriculum revisions, adjustments to teaching workloads, and personnel-related decisions. Educational analytics serves not only as a technical tool but also as a social mechanism for transforming institutional practices. Power BI functions not merely as a monitoring instrument but as an autonomous agent within managerial discourse, initiating shifts in the logic of educational design. Prospective research directions include analyzing institutional resistance to analytical practices and examining the readiness of administrators for data-driven decision-making.

TI CAFE-AI: a synergistic approach to integrating pedagogy and technology
   for AI literacy and growth mindset
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE E-learning; CAFE model; artificial intelligence; AI literacy; growth
   mindset; instructional design; personalized learning
ID HIGHER-EDUCATION; DESIGN
AB This study addresses the need to prepare students for a technology-driven future by developing and testing the CAFE-AI model, an e-learning framework that integrates pedagogy with artificial intelligence (AI). The model operationalizes four components Content, Activity, Facilitation, and Evaluation (CAFE) through three AI paradigms: AI-directed, AI-supported, and AI-empowered. A research and development (R&D) approach was employed, including design, validation, and empirical testing in a university course. Validation involved subject matter and media experts, while effectiveness was examined using AI literacy and growth mindset instruments. Data were analyzed using descriptive statistics and MANOVA. Results from expert evaluation and a pilot study (n = 80) indicated that the model is highly valid and feasible. A quasi-experimental study (n = 152) demonstrated that CAFE-AI is significantly more effective than conventional e-learning in improving AI literacy and growth mindset (p < 0.05). These findings suggest that the CAFE-AI model provides an adaptive and effective framework for fostering essential digital competencies and promoting a growth-oriented mindset in higher education.

TI Dialogic interactions and student agency: a mixed-methods analysis of a
   Chinese primary school classroom guided by knowledge-building principles
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Dialogic interaction; student agency; classroom discourse analysis;
   teacher scaffolding; lag sequential analysis; Chinese primary education;
   Classroom Practice; Classroom Practice
ID WAIT TIME; AUTHORITY
AB This study investigates the interaction patterns within a primary school classroom in China that was designed around knowledge-building principles. While knowledge-building pedagogy has been extensively studied in Western contexts, its implementation in Confucian-heritage classrooms remains underexplored. Using a mixed-methods approach, we developed and applied a 17-category Multimodal Interaction Coding Scheme (MICS) to analyze video recordings of four third-grade batik-themed lessons (N = 1,872 behavioral segments). Quantitative and sequential analyses revealed that student-led discourse dominated classroom activity (62.77%), primarily sustained through peer collaboration (27.40%) and student presentations (26.71%). Teachers primarily used open-ended questioning and indirect guidance (indirect/direct ratio = 0.675) to scaffold learning. Technology served mainly as a presentational and procedural tool, while periods of 'productive silence' functioned as metacognitive spaces yet were seldom leveraged for communal knowledge advancement. The study contributes a validated analytical framework (MICS) and empirical insights into how student agency and dialogic teaching can be effectively realized in a Chinese classroom setting, challenging stereotypes about passive knowledge transmission in Confucian-heritage education.

TI Enhancing communicative competences by using authentic materials in
   Swedish foreign language teaching: a case study of teachers' experiences
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Authentic teaching materials; foreign language teaching; communicative
   competences; receptive skills; productive skills
AB The benefits of the use of authentic materials in foreign language teaching have been recognized in research. In contrast to coursebooks, which often simplify language and focus on grammatical structures, authentic materials provide opportunities to develop communicative language competences. The aim of this qualitative case study is to examine the various methods that foreign language teachers in Sweden employ to integrate authentic materials into their teaching to enhance students' communicative competences. The study addresses the following questions: How do teachers define authentic materials? In what ways do teachers use authentic materials as part of their instructional methods to promote the receptive and productive language skills? What challenges do teachers experience when using authentic materials? Semi-structured interviews were conducted with four teachers, whose responses were analysed thematically. The model of communicative competences developed by Celce-Murcia and Olshtain is used as a theoretical underpinning. Based on teachers' experiences, the findings show how various authentic materials could engage students and enhance their sociocultural, linguistic, and strategic competences. The study underscores the need for clearer guidelines to support teachers in using authentic materials. The findings have implications for aligning classroom practices with educational policy and fostering engagement in language learning through real-world contexts.

TI Mapping the unseen: a data-driven visualization framework for curriculum
   analysis
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Curriculum Mapping; Curriculum Evaluation; Learning Outcomes; Curriculum
   Audit; Program Assessment; Data Visualization; Quality Assurance
ID EDUCATION; TOOL
AB Curriculum mapping is essential for ensuring coherence and constructive alignment across learning outcomes, assessments and educational standards. Yet, the inherent complexity of curricula often turns this process into a time-consuming, technically demanding task, generating outputs that are comprehensive but rarely intuitive or actionable for educators, designers, accreditors, or institutional reviewers. To address these limitations, this study introduces a novel, visualization-driven framework that integrates flow analysis, coverage and alignment matrices, and hierarchical structural analysis into a unified system. Grounded in established pedagogical principles and informed by data visualization theory, the framework offers both qualitative interpretations and quantitative diagnostics through metrics such as the Curriculum Redundancy Index (CRI) and Curriculum Alignment Score (CAS). A suite of interactive, web-based applications were purpose-built to operationalize the framework, providing real-time, filter-driven exploration of complex curricular relationships without requiring advanced technical expertise. The publicly accessible curricular explorer platform (https://apps.odc.edu.om/cmap/demo/) demonstrates these capabilities using a synthetic but structurally realistic dataset that mirrors a full undergraduate program. The system enables users to trace curricular progression, identify misalignment and explore hierarchical depth through qualitative visual metaphors and quantitative indicators. This enhances curriculum mapping's accuracy and usability, transforming it from static documentation into a dynamic, interactive process that supports rapid audits, evidence-based decisions and continuous quality assurance across disciplines.

TI Socioeconomic, demographic, and locational predictors of households'
   educational investment in Somalia
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Household educational investment; socioeconomic determinants;
   multinomial logistic regression; Somali integrated household budget
   survey (SIHBS); parental education
ID CHILDREN; INEQUALITIES; EXPENDITURE; PARENT; INCOME; CHINA
AB This study investigates the determinants of household educational investment in Somalia, focusing on economic, demographic, and geographic factors. Using data from a nationally representative sample of 7,212 households from the Somali Integrated Household Budget Survey (SIHBS) 2022, descriptive analysis, correlation diagnostics, and multinomial logistic regression were employed to examine investment patterns. Households were categorized into low (< USD 200), medium (USD 200-400), and high (> USD 400) investors. The results show that household income, parental education, and geographical location are significantly associated with investment levels. Specifically, higher household income and university-educated parents are linked to greater educational spending, while urban households are more likely to invest than rural and nomadic households. The employment status of the household head and the gender of the child also show significant effects, with employed heads and male children being associated with higher investment. Marginal effects further indicate that a USD 100 increase in household income reduces the probability of low investment by 2.3% and increases high investment by 1.5%, with parental education emerging as the strongest determinant, followed by income and geographic access. These findings highlight persistent disparities in household educational investment across Somalia and underscore the need for policies that enhance household income, expand adult education, improve rural and nomadic access to schooling, support employment opportunities, and address gender-based inequalities.

TI Educational and learning resources for gifted education in emerging
   contexts: an Actiotope Model analysis of high-performance trajectories
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Educational and learning capitals; Actiotope Model of Giftedness;
   inclusive gifted education; Colegios de Alto Rendimiento (COAR);
   systemic gifted education; Global South; Social Sciences; Education;
   Behavioral Sciences; Educational Psychology; Cognitive Psychology;
   Motivation
AB Peru's Colegios de Alto Rendimiento (COAR) represent a pioneering selective residential schooling model aimed at nurturing high-performance students from under-resourced conditions. Applying the Actiotope Model of Giftedness (AMG) and the Educational and Learning Capitals (ELC) framework, this study analyzes qualitative data from 18 COAR graduates to elucidate how educational (exogenous) and learning (endogenous) capitals interact to shape talent development trajectories. Findings reveal that social capital, particularly peer diversity and mentoring, and cultural capital, notably English proficiency and the International Baccalaureate framework, are the primary facilitators expanding students' horizons and building resilience, overshadowing infrastructural capitals. Despite the inclusive scholarship model, critical gaps in vocational guidance, mental health support, and abrupt post-graduation social capital loss limit sustainable development. This study extends ELC research into Latin America, illustrating that in resource-constrained Global South contexts, social and cultural capitals can compensate for infrastructural shortfalls, while sole material investment is insufficient. The findings advocate shifting from selective containment toward longitudinal accompaniment through transition bridges, metacognitive instruction, flexible scholarships, and family engagement to ensure resilient, fulfilling trajectories. These insights offer actionable policy guidance for inclusive and sustainable talent development in under-resourced educational systems globally.

TI The accuracy of parent and teacher nominations in gifted identification
   in Saudi Arabia
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Gifted identification; parent nominations; teacher nominations; Saudi
   Arabia; nomination accuracy; Social Sciences; Education; Behavioral
   Sciences; Educational Psychology; Cognitive Psychology; Motivation
ID STUDENTS; RATINGS; PARTICIPATION; PERFORMANCE; PROGRAMS; CHILDREN
AB The identification of gifted students remains a critical challenge for contemporary education systems, particularly in ensuring both accuracy and equity. This study examined the effectiveness of parent and teacher nominations in the identification of gifted students within Saudi Arabia, drawing upon a large archival dataset of 541,358 nominations collected from 2015 to 2025. Using quantitative methods, the study compared nomination accuracy rates and analyzed the moderating effects of student gender and grade level. The results demonstrated that parent nominations yielded a significantly higher accuracy rate (39.6%) than teacher nominations (31.9%), with a statistically significant mean difference of 7.7 percentage points. The analysis also revealed nuanced gender patterns: parents were significantly more accurate in nominating males, whereas teachers were significantly more accurate in nominating females. Furthermore, a strong grade-level effect was observed, with nomination accuracy for both sources more than doubling between the elementary (grades 3-5) and secondary (grades 6-10) levels. These findings highlighted the complementary value of parent and teacher perspectives in capturing diverse expressions of giftedness and underscore the profound influence of demographic factors. The results carried important implications for refining multi-source identification systems, particularly in non-Western contexts, and contribute to ongoing theoretical and practical debates on achieving equitable and equitable gifted education.

TI Institutionalizing sustainability consciousness: higher education
   leadership in Zimbabwe and South Africa
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Global South leadership; sustainability consciousness; theory U
   presencing; EGO-ECO-SEVA; education for sustainable development
AB Zimbabwean and South African higher education leaders confront entrenched political volatility and chronic resource scarcity through innovative sustainability strategies that distinguish Global South leadership from Northern universalism. This qualitative exploratory study systematically examined senior administrators' approaches to integrating sustainability practices within institutions and evaluated their impact on the development of student and faculty consciousness. In-depth interviews with 20 vice-chancellors, deans, and sustainability directors, along with a comprehensive analysis of 35 strategic plans, institutional reports, and policy documents, revealed four interconnected themes operationalizing the Global South Leadership Framework. These themes include the integration of policy curricula, embedding environmental stewardship as an institutional identity, collaborative networks that extend SEVA-oriented partnerships into regional ecosystems, digital platforms serving as equity infrastructure for rural learners, and capacity building, generating self-sustaining faculty cascades that function as human infrastructure. Unlike Northern-centric education for sustainable development models that assume universal scalability, empirically tested interventions such as Theory U presencing workshops and hybrid digital local partnerships successfully transform sustainability discourse into concrete SDG 4.7-aligned practices amid persistent policy fluctuations. The findings position Southern African higher education leadership as a replicable blueprint that systematically addresses Global scalability gaps in the Global South, offering transferable strategies for developing ESD curricula and strengthening institutional operations in diverse, resource-constrained contexts globally.

TI Is it worth publishing Open Access? - the scientific impact of Open
   Access publications in the field of medical education
SO MEDICAL EDUCATION ONLINE
LA English
DT Article
DE Medical education; Open Access; bibliometrics; research article;
   scientific impact
ID EFFECT SIZE; CITATION
AB Introduction: In medical education research, the transfer of knowledge depends heavily on the visibility of scientific publications. Whether Open Access (OA) actually increases this visibility through a citation advantage (Open Access Citation Advantage, OACA) is still unclear for the field of medical education. This study aims to determine the existence and size of an OACA for medical education articles indexed in PubMed between 2010 and 2019. Method: In a retrospective bibliometric cohort study, all entries classified as research articles by iCite (R) with the MeSH term 'Medical Education' (N = 43,275) were analysed. OA was defined by the presence of a PubMed Central identifier (PMCID). Primary endpoints were total citations, citations per year, and the Relative Citation Ratio (RCR). Group comparisons were made using Yuen-Welch t-tests (20 % trimmed means, alpha = 0.05). Sensitivity analyses included negative binomial regression with year and journal fixed effects, and quantile regression for RCR. Results: 21.2% of articles had a PMCID. PMCID-indexed publications showed higher trimmed means for total citations (10.31 vs. 7.00), annual citations (1.29 vs. 0.79), and RCR (0.79 vs. 0.49; all p < .001). Robust effect sizes (delta(AKP)(R), Algina-Keselman-Penfield robust standardized difference) ranged from 0.27 to 0.35 (small to medium). Negative binomial models with year and journal fixed effects confirmed these findings (IRR range: 1.53-1.67). Conclusions: PMCID-indexed articles in the field of medical education are cited significantly more frequently and have higher field-normalised impact values than non-OA publications. Despite financial hurdles and methodological limitations including potential selection bias, the moderate OACA supports strategically expanding OA publication funds to maximise the reach and impact of medical education research. Prospective studies should consider different OA types, altmetrics, and potential confounders to pinpoint the impacts of OA.

TI Creativity matters, synergies between Spanish ICT sector and higher
   education performance
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE ICT; engineering; higher education; employability; technology
   management; business competitiveness; ICT; engineering; higher
   education; employability; technology management; business
   competitiveness
ID SKILLS
AB This study examines the alignment between skills taught at Spanish technological universities and the demands of the national ICT sector over 15 years, spanning from 2008 to 2023. Key findings indicate that teamwork and problem-solving have consistently been the most valued skills, followed by planning and communication. Focusing on the evolution of the university's role in training key ICT skills, recent graduates have shown a decline in communication skills, teamwork, and proactivity, while improving in problem-solving, planning, and analytical thinking. The study highlights the need to incorporate management skills into ICT curricula, as promoting engineers to managerial positions remains challenging. Additionally, the ICT sector is shifting its priorities, with creativity and innovation becoming more highly valued than customer orientation. The findings emphasise the necessity for educational programs to adapt continuously to meet industry needs and enhance graduate employability, particularly in a new business scenario where technology management is increasingly driving global competitive strategies.

TI False promises and false hopes: credentialization and games education
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Credentialism; digital/video games; professionalization; curriculum;
   education-job mismatch
AB Many educational institutions now offer credentials in game development/design and game studies to meet perceived industry demands and cater to students' employment aspirations. However, graduates have difficulty finding relevant employment. These programs differ fundamentally in their purpose and curricula, and their quality and worth are debated (Ashton, 2009; Keogh & Hardwick, 2024). We use credentialization and signaling theory to suggest that the value associated with games credentials may be abstracted from the skills required for successful games-related careers, rendering credentials unclear value signals to students and potential employers (Spence, 1973). To interrogate this proposition, this study examined the signaling strength and valuation of a games credential by triangulating interview data from three key stakeholder groups: graduates (n = 207), games program educators (n = 12) and games industry professionals (n = 42). Using a grounded theory approach to interview analysis, we identified three interrelated themes which collectively point to misperceptions and differential valuations of games credentials and a fourth theme that identifies challenges to change. This study is one of the first to problematize the credentialization of the games industry and to offer practical recommendations for partnership development between educators and industry professionals.

TI Curriculum reform and practice of a graduate-level course based on the
   CDIO engineering education model: a case study of Pattern
   recognition
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE CDIO engineering education; graduate curriculum reform; pattern
   recognition; programming practice; academic writing; Arts & Humanities;
   Arts; Media Communication; Humanities; Cultural Studies; Media & Film
   Studies; Media & Communications; Communication Studies
AB This paper examines the current state of graduate education in the discipline of Control Science and Engineering, as well as the Control Engineering professional master's program at our university. It identifies key shortcomings in graduate-level courses, particularly regarding the integration of theory and practice, the cultivation of research capabilities, and academic writing training. To address these issues, this paper proposes and explores concrete reform measures and practices based on the Conceive-Design-Implement-Operate (CDIO) engineering education model, taking the Pattern Recognition course as a case study. The reform was implemented on 235 graduate students, with its effectiveness evaluated by a mixed method of course assessments and classroom observations. Key results show that the proportion of students achieving excellent performance, which is above 90 points, increased from 19.1% in the 2022-2023 cohort (i.e. before the reform) to 48.3% in the 2023-2024 cohort (i.e. after the reform), the average score rose from 86.9 to 88.9, and classroom observations consistently indicated high engagement and participation. In addition, over ten course papers were recommended for presentation at university-level academic conferences. The research findings suggest that the CDIO-based teaching approach has achieved remarkable results in enhancing students' practical competence, research capabilities, and academic writing skills. This CDIO-integrated teaching paradigm can serve as a reference for teaching reform in other engineering disciplines at the university, as well as in engineering disciplines at other universities that aim to strengthen the connection between theoretical learning and engineering practice.

TI Perceived career success and career advancement of women: challenges in
   Nigerian higher education institutions
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Mentoring; organizational support; family responsibilities; career
   success; career advancement; social role theory; Social Sciences;
   Education; Higher Education; Study of Higher Education; Study of Higher
   Educatio
ID ORGANIZATIONAL SUPPORT; FAMILY RESPONSIBILITIES; PERFORMANCE;
   EXPERIENCES; MANAGERIAL; ACADEMIA; GENDER; WORK
AB This study examines factors influencing perceived career success (PCS) and career advancement (CA) among women academics in Nigerian higher education institutions, focusing on mentoring, perceived organizational support (POS), and family responsibilities (FR). Guided by Social Role Theory, six hypotheses were tested on the direct relationships between these variables and PCS and CA. Data were collected through an online survey and analyzed using SmartPLS 4. Findings reveal that mentoring significantly predicts both PCS and CA, underscoring its critical role in career advancement. Similarly, POS positively influences PCS and CA, highlighting the importance of supportive institutional environments. While FR significantly affects CA, it has no significant impact on PCS. The study provides insights into how institutional and personal factors shape women's career outcomes and emphasizes the need for policies that strengthen mentorship and organizational support in academia.

TI 'Active engagement instead of sitting around': how a serious game can
   enhance learning for organizational climate adaptation
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Game-based learning; transformative learning; climate adaptation;
   organizational learning; grounded theory; Social Sciences; Education;
   Adult Education and Lifelong Learning; Work-based Learning; Philosophy
   of Education; Research Methods in Education
ID ROLE-PLAY SIMULATIONS; GAMIFICATION; METHODOLOGY; EDUCATION; IMPACTS
AB Climate adaptation requires profound transformations in organizational thinking and acting, yet organizational members often lack orientation and learning opportunities to understand and enact their roles and responsibilities in these processes. Games offer a non-threatening environment in which complex issues such as climate change can be explored in a practice-oriented way. The competitive nature of games is known to increase participants' motivation and evoke emotional engagement. In this study, game-based learning is compared with more traditional learning formats, and its effectiveness is examined. For this purpose, 16 expert interviews were conducted with climate managers from industry and climate change lecturers following their participation in a game specifically designed for this study. The findings highlight the effectiveness of participatory and interactive formats: even participants with extensive expertise were encouraged by the game setting and the heterogeneous group of players to reflect on their climate-relevant behavior in both their private and professional lives. The Grounded Theory analysis also emphasizes the importance of neutral moderation: moderators must be able to reconcile different motivations, emotions, and values. A detailed debriefing after the game was likewise identified as essential, as it provides an opportunity to reflect on new insights and experiences and to situate them within a broader context. Overall, the study underscores the need for innovative approaches to professional development that can foster changes in the mental models of both individual employees and organizations and support the re-evaluation of existing beliefs and practices. The transformative learning that can occur in a protected gaming environment offers particular potential for improvisation, creative thinking, reflection, problem-solving, and cooperation.

TI The role of competence and autonomy support in instructor motivation: a
   path model in Norwegian higher education
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Higher education; instructors; autonomy support; competence; intrinsic
   motivation
ID SELF-DETERMINATION THEORY; TEACHERS MOTIVATION;
   PROFESSIONAL-DEVELOPMENT; INTRINSIC MOTIVATION; STUDENTS MOTIVATION;
   MEDIATING ROLE; WORK; ASSOCIATIONS; LEADERSHIP; CLIMATE
AB Higher education institutions increasingly emphasize autonomy-supportive teaching, yet little is known about how instructors' perceived competence and the autonomy support they receive from institutional leadership jointly shape their motivation and teaching-related intentions. Drawing on Self-Determination Theory, this study addresses this gap by examining how perceived autonomy support and competence relate to intrinsic motivation, external regulation, autonomy-supportive teaching behaviors, and teaching intentions among university instructors. Using national survey data from 136 biology instructors in Norway, we tested a path model assessing these relationships. Structural equation modeling showed that perceived competence strongly predicted intrinsic motivation, which in turn positively predicted teaching intentions, while external regulation negatively predicted teaching intentions. Contrary to theoretical expectations, perceived autonomy support from leadership was unrelated to intrinsic motivation or external regulation. Competence also directly predicted autonomy-supportive teaching behaviors, whereas motivational pathways did not. The results indicate that competence acts as a central driver of instructor motivation. The findings suggest that faculty development initiatives that strengthen instructors' perceived teaching competence may be a particularly effective lever for enhancing motivation and teaching intentions in higher education.

TI Course design, mentorship, skills, and students' entrepreneurial
   aspirations: evidence from Maldives HEIs
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Higher education; entrepreneurial aspirations; student perceptions;
   experiential learning; graduate employability
ID EDUCATION; MODELS
AB The research aims to explore the impact of university courses, skills and capabilities, and mentorship on students' entrepreneurial aspirations. The study employed a quantitative survey design, utilizing an online survey to collect data from students enrolled in higher education institutions (HEIs) in the Maldives. The study employed confirmatory factor analysis and structural equation modeling, to test the hypotheses. The findings reveal that only skills and capability have a significantly positive impact on entrepreneurial aspiration among the students. In addition, the course has a significantly positive impact on entrepreneurial skills and capability among the students, while there is also a significantly positive impact of course on mentorship effectiveness. These findings indicate an indirect pathway: course design builds skills and fosters mentorship, while accumulated skills ultimately translate into aspirations. This study highlights the need for HEIs to integrate comprehensive entrepreneurship programs that include practical mentorship and skill-building opportunities. The main limitation of the study is that the sample is relatively small and cannot be generalizable to all students in the HEIs of the Maldives. Also, the study is context-specific to the Maldives, which limits the applicability of findings to other cultural settings.

TI Gifted education in China: an analysis from an educational and learning
   resource perspective
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Gifted education; educational capitals; learning capitals; Social
   Sciences; Education; Education Policy & Politics; Educational
   Psychology; Inclusion and Special Educational Needs
ID GROWTH MINDSET; STUDENTS; RESILIENCE; PARADIGM; PROGRAMS
AB In recent years, as the nation has prioritized the cultivation of top-tier innovative talents, gifted education has gained increasing prominence in China's educational system. Existing research has focused mainly on student selection, curriculum reform, or case-based practices, with limited use of systematic frameworks to examine its overall ecology and resource structure. This study applies Ziegler's Actiotope Model of Giftedness and analyzes gifted education in China through ten types of educational and learning capitals. Using secondary analysis of policy documents, statistical data, and authoritative studies from 2010 to 2025, it reviews the resource structure and developmental challenges of gifted education. Findings show that financial input has risen but dedicated funding is lacking, social support networks are emerging yet limited, cultural values are diversifying but unstable, infrastructural digitalization is progressing yet urban-rural disparities persist, and teaching reforms are advancing though professionalization remains weak. In terms of learning capitals, students demonstrate strong actional performance, while organismic support is uneven, goal setting is influenced by utilitarian orientations, episodic transfer and interdisciplinary application remain underdeveloped, and attentional resources risk depletion under heavy workloads. Overall, gifted education has advanced under national policies and local practices but faces persistent challenges of uneven resource allocation, insufficient institutional support, and inadequate early identification. To address these issues, the paper proposes optimization strategies at four levels: orientation, identification, basic principles, and resource management, providing theoretical foundations and policy guidance for the improvement and sustainable development of gifted education in China.

TI Linking inclusive dispositions to teaching competency: the mediating
   role of self-development among preschool teachers
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Inclusive education disposition; inclusive teaching competency;
   self-development; preschool teachers; structural equation modeling;
   Social Sciences; Education; Teachers & Teacher Education; Higher
   Education; Assessment; Multicultural Education
ID EFFICACY
AB Inclusive education is a key component of equitable early childhood systems, yet its implementation relies heavily on teachers' beliefs, attitudes, and professional capacities. In China's rapidly expanding preschool sector, the development of teachers' inclusive teaching competency remains insufficiently examined. This study investigates how preschool teachers' inclusive education disposition (IED) influences their inclusive teaching competency (ITC), with self-development (SD) functioning as a mediating mechanism. A cross-sectional survey was administered to 425 preschool teachers across 16 districts in Shanghai, and partial least squares structural equation modeling (PLS-SEM) was used to assess the measurement and structural models. The results show that IED significantly predicts ITC both directly and indirectly, while SD emerges as the strongest proximal predictor of ITC (beta = 0.80, p < .001). The structural model accounts for 84% of the variance in ITC (R-2 = 0.84), highlighting the central role of teachers' goal setting, reflective engagement, and autonomous learning in translating inclusive dispositions into effective teaching practices. Conceptually, the study clarifies the distinct role of SD-as a developmental and reflective process rather than a belief construct-in linking dispositions with competencies. Practically, the findings suggest that strengthening teachers' self-directed learning, reflective practice, and long-term professional growth opportunities is essential for sustaining improvements in inclusive teaching within early childhood education settings.

TI What factors influence health professions trainees' preferences for
   employment in rural Appalachia? a qualitative study to inform the design
   of a discrete choice experiment
SO MEDICAL EDUCATION ONLINE
LA English
DT Article
DE Qualitative research; rural health services; education; professional;
   economics; medical
ID REMOTE AREAS; DOCTORS; STUDENTS; INCENTIVES; PHYSICIANS; INDONESIA;
   POLICY
AB Optimizing the geographic allocation of health care providers has the potential to improve health care access and health outcomes for rural populations. To inform the design of a discrete choice experiment (DCE) survey to elicit health professions trainees' preferences for rural practice, this formative research aimed to: (1) elicit factors that influence job selection among health profession trainees, including medical residents physician assistant (PA) and nurse practitioner (NP) students, (2) identify how these trainees evaluate trade-offs for rural job placement and (3) inform attribute selection for inclusion into a future DCE to identify health professions trainees' preferences for rural practice. Third- and fourth-year medical residents, PA students, and NP students completed an in-depth semi-structured interview, an adaptive choice-based conjoint exercise, and a ranking exercise. Inductive and deductive coding and reflexive thematic analysis were used to analyze the data. We reached thematic saturation after 22 interviews (n = 14 PA, n = 6 residents, n = 2 NP). We identified multiple domains across two major themes: (1) job-related attributes and (2) community-related attributes. Job-related attribute domains included: (1) team dynamics, management/leadership, and clinical decision support, (2) onboarding and professional development, and (3) compensation package/remuneration. Community-related covered two attribute domains included: (1) amenities and social environment and (2) rurality and commute time. Participants were willing to trade non-pecuniary factors against salary and loan repayment. We identified 10 attributes for potential inclusion in the DCE. This study identified several viable factors for potential inclusion as attributes in a DCE. Several of these factors have yet to be explored in a policy environment and may aid the design of rural healthcare recruitment strategies.

TI Student voices from an under-researched context: the relative impact of
   teacher quality and instructional leadership on school environment in
   Somaliland
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Student voice; teacher quality; instructional leadership; school
   environment; Somaliland; educational effectiveness; transformational
   leadership
AB This study examines how teacher quality and instructional leadership shape the school environment in Somaliland through student voice. Guided by Educational Effectiveness perspectives and Transformational/Instruction-oriented leadership traditions, we model the school environment as a proximal outcome of teaching, with leadership effects expected through visible, instruction-facing routines. A survey of students from seven public secondary schools (N = 373) was analysed using multiple linear regression. Predictors included Perceived Teacher Quality & Practice (PTQP) and four leadership routines (prioritising instructional time, principal curriculum awareness, clarity of vision, principal presence). The outcome was Perceived School Environment & Resources (PSER). The model explained 45% of variance in PSER (R & sup2; = .45, p < .001). PTQP was the dominant predictor (beta = .55, p < .001). Two leadership routines-principal curriculum awareness (beta = .13, p = .003) and principal presence (beta = .09, p = .022)-were significant; others were not. Findings confirm teaching's proximal dominance for students' experience and show that in centralized, resource-constrained settings, leadership "travels" through concrete, curriculum-focused behaviours students can see. Policy should prioritise classroom-embedded professional learning and routinise visible instructional leadership. The study demonstrates the value of student-reported indicators for evaluating school conditions in fragile contexts.

TI The Janus face of AI literacy: a dual-pathway model of its mitigating
   and exacerbating effects on academic procrastination
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Academic procrastination; AI literacy; perfectionism; academic
   integrity; academic self-efficacy; social cognitive theory; cognitive
   load theory; Social Sciences; Behavioral Sciences; Behavioral
   Neuroscience; Cognitive Neuroscience; Cognitive Neuroscience of
   Language; Developmental Psychology; Language Development; Infancy
ID PERFECTIONISM
AB The rapid integration of generative artificial intelligence (GenAI) into higher education has created a paradoxical landscape, offering unprecedented access to knowledge while simultaneously posing risks for academic misconduct and procrastination. This dynamic is particularly salient in contexts lacking formal regulatory frameworks, where student behavior is primarily governed by individual psychological and competency-based factors. This study investigates the mediating role of AI literacy in the relationship between key psychological determinants reflected as perfectionism, academic integrity as well as academic self-efficacy and academic procrastination. Grounded in Bandura's Social Cognitive Theory and Cognitive Load Theory, the research employs a cross-sectional survey design conducted from April to June 2025. Data from 654 Indonesian higher education students, recruited through quota sampling in an unmandated GenAI environment, were analyzed using SEM (SmartPLS). Validated instruments measured academic procrastination, AI literacy, perfectionism, academic integrity, and academic self-efficacy. Results indicate that perfectionism predicts procrastination, while academic integrity and self-efficacy are negative predictors. AI literacy partially mediates these relationships, demonstrating a dual role in mitigating and, under conditions, exacerbating procrastination. These findings highlight AI literacy not only as a technical competence but also as a psychological resource for managing cognitive load and supporting adaptive learning behavior in digital educational ecosystems.

TI A psychological approach to music composition pedagogy: analytical
   insights from 'clear autumn sky'
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Music composition pedagogy; psychological approach; creativity; clear
   autumn sky; student voice; gender differences; Music composition
   pedagogy; psychological approach; creativity; clear autumn sky; student
   voice; gender differences
ID ENGAGEMENT; CREATIVITY
AB This study examines how to systematically incorporate psychological aspects in music composition teaching by using 'Clear Autumn Sky' as a model that affecting how students compose music and their outcomes. A cohort of 35 students (23 males, 12 females) from Years 7-9 participated in initial surveys and long-term self-evaluation through longitudinal video portfolios, utilizing a mixed-methods experimental design. A control group of 32 students (19 males, 13 females) of equivalent age participated in identical video-based self-evaluation activities. The findings indicate that the psychological approach significantly enhances students' notational fluency, creativity, craftsmanship, and aesthetic sensitivity. Male students demonstrated greater success in collaborative settings, while female students responded more favorably to emotionally nuanced environments. Gender-based trends were observed. The results suggest that using psychological methods relevant to the situation can significantly increase overall compositional skills in school. This study presents a new way for music teachers to consider reconciling individual expression with group innovation in psychologically rich learning contexts.

   attitudes, and transformative digital resources
SO COGENT EDUCATION

DT Article

ID STUDENTS; AI
AB Generative Artificial Intelligence (GAI) is reshaping higher education, showing early evidence of potential to optimize teaching and learning processes through personalized support, content creation, and assessment automation. However, its integration into teacher training presents challenges in terms of perception, attitudes, and intention to use. This study analyzes the impact of implementing GAI tools in the training of preservice teachers, assessing their influence on the perception of their educational value and their willingness to adopt them. Incorporating GAI as an innovative methodology in teacher training seams to increase their confidence and security in its use, recommending the development of training strategies that enhance its critical and effective adoption in the educational field.

TI Making connections: teacher perspectives on interdisciplinarity in
   physical education and mathematics
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Interdisciplinarity; curriculum; mathematics; physical education; middle
   years
ID CURRICULUM INTEGRATION; SCHOOL
AB This article investigates interdisciplinary teaching practices that integrate mathematics and physical education in middle school contexts, drawing on two original case studies conducted in a German international school and an Australian public school. Both schools' curricula emphasised the integration of mathematical concepts with physical education content, particularly through lessons involving data analysis, measurement and fitness-related activities. Framing the study within Klein's principles of successful interdisciplinarity, emphasizing transparency, systematic integration, boundary crossing and recognition of collaborative work, this research examines how teachers navigate interdisciplinary collaboration. Using an embedded dual-case study design with semi-structured interviews, the study explored how interdisciplinary approaches influence teaching efficacy, curriculum coherence and instructional strategies. Analysis of the data revealed four key themes: transparency and communication, consistency and systematic alignment, balance of disciplinary and interdisciplinary integration, and recognition and resourcing of interdisciplinary teaching. Findings highlight the critical role of teacher agency, the importance of collaboration, and boundary-crossing strategies, alongside logistical and structural factors in facilitating successful and meaningful integration of mathematics and physical education. The study offers practical implications for middle school teachers that can help promote interdisciplinary initiatives, including the need for prior strategic planning, professional development and a balanced approach to institutional support to enhance interdisciplinary teaching in middle years education.

TI Lecturer competence and internship programmes as catalysts for
   developing agricultural education graduates' competencies for the world
   of work
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Lecturer competencies; student internships; graduate competencies;
   workforce; partnerships; business sector; Lecturer competencies; student
   internships; graduate competencies; workforce; partnerships; business
   sector
ID STUDENTS; READINESS
AB This study investigates how higher education institutions can reduce unemployment by aligning graduate competencies with market demands. This study focuses on the influence of lecturer competencies and internship programmes in preparing graduates for the world of work. This study used cross-sectional design with structured questionnaires distributed to 165 third-year agribusiness and agrotechnology students from UPN Veteran East Java and Universitas Wijaya Kusuma Surabaya, selected through stratified and systematic sampling. Data were analysed using Structural Equation Modelling to explore relationships between lecturer competencies, internship performance and graduate competency achievement. The results show that lecturers' pedagogical, professional and personal competencies are positively correlated with internship performance with coefficients of 0.331, 0.183 and 0.252 vice Versa and with graduate competency achievement with coefficients of 0.144, 0.186 and 0.024, respectively. Internship performance strongly correlates with graduate competency achievement with a coefficient of 0.360. Social competence is indirectly linked through internship performance. The study concludes that lecturer competency development, strengthened through targeted training and strong industry partnerships are essential. Internship programmes should be integrated into curricula and made compulsory to enhance practical skills and employability in agriculture-related fields.

TI From work attributes to engagement: the crucial role of work-life
   balance for secondary school teachers in Tanzania
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Employees work engagement; work attributes; work-life balance; secondary
   school teachers; Tanzania; Teachers & Teacher Education; Teacher
   Education & Development
ID PERFORMANCE
AB This study examined how work-life balance moderates the relationship between work attributes and work engagement among secondary school teachers in Tanzania. It investigated whether teachers' ability to balance professional and personal demands influences their level of passion and commitment to their roles. A cross-sectional research design was adopted, with data collected using a structured questionnaire from 198 secondary school teachers, selected through stratified random sampling. Structural equation modeling (SEM) was used for analysis. Results informed that, work attributes significantly and positively predict work engagement. Notably, work-life balance strengthened this relationship: teachers who effectively managed work and personal life exhibited higher engagement, particularly when their jobs offered favorable attributes. A significant positive direct link was also found between work-life balance and work engagement, indicating that better balance fosters greater job involvement. This study extends Social Exchange Theory by demonstrating that organizational support for work life balance is perceived as a positive exchange, prompting employees to reciprocate with increased engagement. It contributes to the literature by highlighting the moderating role of work-life balance in enhancing teacher engagement, offering insights for educational institutions aiming to improve motivation and performance
C1 [Kibambila, Vincent Kadoke] Univ Dodoma, Coll Business & Econ, Dept Business Adm & Management, Dodoma, Tanzania.

TI Primary teachers' responses to municipal digital investments
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Municipal policy; teachers; policy enactment; focus groups;
   ethnomethodology; Arts & Humanities; Arts; Media Communication;
   Humanities; Media & Film Studies; Media & Communications; Media
   Education; Social Sciences; Behavioral Sciences; Educational Psychology
ID POLICY
AB This study examines primary school teachers as policy actors through focus group conversations addressing a municipal investment lasting for ten years. This case illuminates how international and national policy is interpreted in municipal policy, aiming to implement one-computer-one-student (1-1) teaching. The data is approached through the lens of policy enactment and ethnomethodology, with special attention to how policy is enacted in local practices. Two common patterns in our data are scrutinised: focus groups for the investment and focus groups against the investment. A third focus group in which teachers disputed the investment is also reported on. In relation to earlier studies, ours shows how the enactment of the municipal policy depends on how the repertoires are shaped and developed in the local context of teachers' work. It is concluded that if educational policy is to be understood in full, the variety of ways in which teachers respond to and enact policy should be documented and scrutinised.

TI Biology teachers' professional learning at school: opportunities and
   challenges in Tanzania
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Professional development; school-based professional learning;
   standardised training; biology; Tanzania; Professional development;
   school-based professional learning; standardised training; biology;
   Tanzania
AB The quality of education is directly linked to the quality of teachers, which is significantly influenced by pre-service and continuous professional development (CPD). In Tanzania, CPD has faced challenges such as irregular training, limited funding, and poor coordination, prompting a shift towards school-based professional learning (SBPL). This study examines biology teachers' understanding, engagement, challenges, and opportunities for SBPL. The study employed a mixed-methods with a concurrent design; data were collected through questionnaires and semi-structured interviews. Findings reveal that biology teachers have varied insights about SBPL. As a result, their engagement in school-based learning remains low. The study identifies key challenges, including a lack of time for teacher learning, unstructured SBPL programs, and inadequate resources. However, strong communication networks, committed teachers, resource centres, and ongoing curriculum reforms present opportunities to improve SBPL. Teachers should be informed about SBPL, and incorporating digital skills training into professional development can enhance their use of technology. Recommendations have been proposed to help improve the current situation.

TI Finnish teacher trainers' transnational teaching experiences in
   education export programmes for Chinese teachers: an intercultural
   perspective
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE China; education export; Finland; in-service teacher training;
   intercultural experiences; teacher education; transnational teaching
   experiences; Social Sciences; Education
AB Over the past decade, Finnish in-service teacher training programmes have been exported to Chinese teachers. Little has been known about how Finnish teacher trainers experience these programmes. The current study seeks to examine the transnational teaching experiences of Finnish teacher trainers in these programmes, with a particular focus on the intercultural aspect. In this study, four Finnish teacher trainers were interviewed using a semi-structured approach with narrative characteristics to elicit their accounts of intercultural experiences while training Chinese teachers through their education export services. The interviews were analysed using inductive content analysis through the theoretical lens of Holliday's Grammar of Culture. The findings indicate that the interviewees articulated their intercultural experiences primarily through their Finnish and personal positionalities while recognising the nuances of diversity within the Chinese society and school system. The interviewees encountered several challenges when training Chinese teachers, particularly about language barriers, communication issues, and the ambiguous dynamics of each training group. By modifying their pedagogical approaches and behaviour, they were able to surmount these challenges. Despite these challenges, they have benefited from enriching their professional practice, and broadening horizons and their professional identity.

TI Preservice teachers' perceptions of AI- and human-generated feedback on
   lesson plans
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Preservice teachers; AI (peer) review; Effective feedback; Guided
   reading; Teacher preparation
AB This study examines the role of AI peer reviewers in evaluating preservice teachers' guided reading lesson plans at a U.S. university. A quasi-experimental design compared two groups: one receiving traditional human peer reviews and another using ChatGPT as a peer reviewer. Data sources included peer review reports and student survey responses. Grounded in the Zone of Proximal Development and effective feedback literature, the study investigates how AI feedback influences critical thinking and engagement during lesson planning. It also explores preservice teachers' attitudes toward AI versus human feedback. The findings demonstrate both the advantages and disadvantages of AI-generated feedback which provides valuable information about its effectiveness and trustworthiness in teacher preparation. The study adds to the discussion about implementing AI in teaching methods and the changing nature of peer review in teacher training programs.

TI Human-machine cognition in relation to computational linguistics course
   learning: an empirical study of linguistics majors
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Human-machine cognition; explicit cognition; implicit association test
   (IAT); computational linguistics course; linguistics majors; Behavioral
   Sciences; Cognitive Psychology
ID IMPLICIT
AB Generative AI challenges the traditional view of humanity centred on language/rationality and impacts language-related majors. This study investigates the relationship between taking a 'Computational Linguistics' course and the evolution of explicit and implicit human-machine cognition among Chinese Linguistics majors. Through a longitudinal controlled experiment combining questionnaires and the Implicit Association Test (IAT), it explores how the acquisition of technological knowledge interacts with and reshapes perceptions of human uniqueness. The results indicate that course learning did not alter university students' factual understanding of human-machine attributes, nor did it change their value judgments regarding human distinctive attributes and shared attributes. At the level of implicit cognition, students generally exhibited a strong association between humans and distinctive attributes, and the strength of this association was not affected by course learning. However, course performance correlated with variations in the salience of this association. Students with higher academic achievement showed a more pronounced implicit human distinctive attribute association than those with lower academic performance.

TI A STEAM integrated middle school unit about insects in the garden:
   friends and foes
SO SCIENCE ACTIVITIES-PROJECTS AND CURRICULUM IDEAS IN STEM CLASSROOMS
LA English
DT Article
DE STEAM-integration; middle-school; insects; biological-control; 3-H
   model; garden-based
AB This article presents an integrated STEAM (science, technology, engineering, art, and math) unit designed for middle school students, centered on the ecological roles of insects in organic gardens. Using the 3-H (Hearts-on, Hands-on, Heads-on) Social and Emotional Learning Cycle, the unit guides students through the exploration of arthropod anatomy, insect classification, and the dynamics of biological control. Students engage in inquiry-based activities, including nature journaling, 3-D model construction, and the creation of "Bug for Hire" or "Most Wanted Bug" posters to synthesize their understanding of predators, herbivores, and pollinators. By bridging scientific observation with artistic representation, this unit fosters an appreciation for biodiversity and introduces students to sustainable agricultural practices and STEM-related careers, such as entomology.
