TI Computational thinking meets robotics: using mBot2 to explore kinematics
   concepts in physics classrooms
SO SCIENCE ACTIVITIES-PROJECTS AND CURRICULUM IDEAS IN STEM CLASSROOMS
LA English
DT Article
DE educational robotics; computational thinking; mBot2; kinematics; physics
   education
ID EFFICACY
AB Robotics and computational thinking have been increasingly recognized as powerful tools in physics education because they let learners test models, visualize dynamic systems, and practice algorithmic problem-solving in concrete and authentic ways. This article presents a 5-E lesson plan that integrates educational robotics and computational thinking to teach kinematics concepts in classroom settings. It introduces the mBot2 robotics kit and its mBlock programming environment, outlines a detailed 90-minute 5-E sequence with three scaffolded challenges, and aligns the activities to NGSS three-dimensional learning. The lesson plan emphasizes hands-on engagement, inquiry-based learning, and the development of computational thinking skills. The lesson was implemented with 82 preservice science and mathematics teachers during the 2024-2025 academic year and the findings indicated the lesson was engaging, fun, and effective in helping participants link hands-on robotics activities to kinematics concepts (e.g., displacement, velocity, acceleration, motion graphs) while fostering computational thinking skills such as decomposition, pattern recognition, abstraction, and algorithmic thinking. The article concludes by highlighting the practical implications for physics teachers to incorporate robotics-enhanced learning activities in their instruction.

TI Who moved my text? Assessing whether ChatGPT can write better abstracts
   than humans
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Abstracts; ChatGPT; generative AI; readability; comprehensiveness;
   trustworthiness; Abstracts; ChatGPT; generative AI; readability;
   comprehensiveness; trustworthiness
ID COGNITIVE LOAD; ARTIFICIAL-INTELLIGENCE; INSTRUCTIONAL-DESIGN;
   PROCESSING FLUENCY; READABILITY; FAILURE; TONE
AB Purpose: This study investigates, from the readers' perspective, whether ChatGPT can generate research abstracts that are perceived as more readable, comprehensive, trustworthy, and overall better than human-written versions. Design/methodology/approach: Through the lens of Cognitive Load and Processing Fluency theories, it explores how generative artificial intelligence (GenAI) tools may influence readers' assessment of the textual characteristics of accounting and auditing scientific texts. Using a quasi-experimental method, it gathered fifteen qualitative papers from a leading accounting journal, for which their original abstracts were rewritten by ChatGPT in its version 4.0, based on a standardised prompt. Fifteen experienced researchers from Portugal and Brazil evaluated these two versions through a blinded process. The assessment also included content analysis by NVivo and readability indices. Findings: While ChatGPT-generated abstracts are generally preferred, particularly for readability and comprehensiveness, they do not consistently outperform human-written versions, as the messages' perceived trustworthiness appears to support the respondents' judgment. Research limitations/implications: The findings are primarily constrained by a small sample size from only two countries, a limited journal scope, and a single GenAI tool, which limits its generalizability. Besides, the lack of respondents' familiarity with sources and the inclusion of solely expert researchers may have biased the trustworthiness perception. Practical implications: The findings have implications for future uses of GenAI in research dissemination and academic publishing. Originality/value: Despite its constraints, it offers novel insights into the potential applications of GenAI in academia, emphasising that content richness and precision should not be compromised by oversimplification, particularly in technical and scientific disciplines.
C1 [Albuquerque, Fabio; Gomes dos Santos, Paula] Inst Politecn Lisboa, ISCAL, Lisbon, Portugal.
   [Albuquerque, Fabio; Gomes dos Santos, Paula] IPCA, Sch Management, CICF, Barcelos, Portugal.

TI Using immersive virtual reality to enhance students' design thinking
   disposition and academic achievement
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Virtual reality; design thinking disposition; immersive technology;
   academic achievement; electrochemistry; high school
ID CHEMISTRY; TECHNOLOGY; EDUCATION; SCIENCE; ELECTROCHEMISTRY;
   ENVIRONMENT; TEACHERS
AB This study aims to examine the effect of immersive virtual reality (IVR) on students' design thinking disposition and academic achievement in the topic of electrochemistry. This research employed a quantitative approach with a quasi-experimental design, utilizing pre-test and post-test assessments for both the experimental and control groups. A total of 72 twelfth-grade students from two intact classes at a public high school in Jakarta were selected through convenience sampling. Both groups followed the 5E learning cycle; however, the experimental group utilized VR technology (Oculus Quest 2), while the control group relied on PowerPoint presentations and videos. The collected data were analyzed using independent t-tests, paired t-tests, and Pearson's correlation. The results of the t-test revealed that learning electrochemistry through IVR was more effective in enhancing students' design thinking disposition and academic achievement in the experimental group compared to the control group (non-IVR). Also, it was found that students' design thinking disposition and achievement in the experimental group indicated a positive and significant correlation. The findings suggest that IVR can effectively enhance students' design thinking disposition and academic achievement, offering a valuable reference for educators seeking to incorporate VR technology into educational practice.

TI A qualitative follow-up study on academic success of non-traditional
   students
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Non-traditional students; academic success; self-regulated learning;
   qualitative longitudinal study; thematic analysis
ID HIGHER-EDUCATION; LIFELONG LEARNERS; ENGAGEMENT
AB The number of non-traditional students (NTS) has increased in recent decades because of the opening of universities. During their studies, they are often confronted with different challenges than those faced by traditional students. To examine how NTS can be supported on their way to a successful degree, it is important to identify factors that promote and inhibit academic success. Three NTS were interviewed at the beginning of their studies and after two semesters. The interviews were then analysed using thematic analysis. To identify factors contributing to academic success at an individual and more objective level, deductive and inductive approaches were combined. The results indicated that NTS had to make their learning as profitable as possible to be successful in their studies. This is particularly manageable if the study requirements are sufficiently known, learning objectives are defined and appropriate learning strategies are used. Although the sample size was small, we included two measurement points; therefore, we could describe the development of NTS in higher education settings. The importance of clear goals and application of learning strategies can inform further (quantitative) studies.

TI Building a sustainable engineering pipeline: ECSA-academic collaboration
   on reducing attrition
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Sustainability; skills pipeline; sustainable engineering; engineering
   attrition; retention; Engineering Council of South Africa;
   Sustainability; skills pipeline; sustainable engineering; engineering
   attrition; retention; Engineering Council of South Africa
ID HIGHER-EDUCATION; STUDENTS; SUCCESS; EXPERIENCES
AB The engineering profession is central to South Africa's innovation, however, persistently high attrition rates within the skills pipeline threaten the sustainability of the engineering workforce. This study aimed to address high attrition rates in the engineering profession. A mixed-methods research design was used, and data were collected through qualitative surveys and questionnaires. The study included n = 10 purposively sampled academic staff and n = 263 registered engineering candidates sampled using simple random sampling. The qualitative data was analysed using thematic analysis, while the quantitative data was analysed using Statistical Package for the Social Sciences. The study identified foundational gaps in basic education, institutional barriers, and language proficiency as contributors to attrition. Significant differences were found in perceptions of academic workload across candidate categories (H = 21.006, p < 0.001), with Candidate Engineers reporting the greatest difficulty (Mean Rank= 110.26). While no statistically significant differences were observed for financial-related items among the Candidate Engineers (p > 0.05), 80% of lecturers cited financial challenges as a key contributor to attrition. Qualitative insights highlighted that 70% of lecturers identified foundational gaps in mathematics and science, and 60% raised language proficiency as a barrier. The study recommends enhanced collaboration between ECSA and higher education institutions to foster a more sustainable engineering pipeline.

TI A new twist to the tried-and-true egg-drop activity for secondary
   students
SO SCIENCE ACTIVITIES-PROJECTS AND CURRICULUM IDEAS IN STEM CLASSROOMS
LA English
DT Article
DE Force; motion; science practices
AB This secondary level (grades 9-12) lesson links concepts from prior lessons learned about the Egg Drop task from elementary and/or middle school. By the end of the lesson, students should have learned advanced content about forces, momentum, acceleration, and velocity. More importantly, students should have a conceptual understanding of mechanical phenomena. The lesson structure is not entirely project-based or inquiry-based; rather, some limited aspects of direct instruction are present whereby the teacher should employ close monitoring, guided practice, and independent practice when students calculate force, momentum, energy, and velocity quantities. Apart from the mathematical components of the lesson, the instructional sequence for this five-day lesson is primarily project-based (ex. task specific) and inquiry-based (ex. students explore and then explain).

TI Investigating the impact of Kahoot! on the ESP vocabulary knowledge
   development of Chinese EFL learners: a quasi-experimental study
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE EFL; ESP vocabulary knowledge; game-based learning; gamification;
   Kahoot!
ID 2ND LANGUAGE; DEPTH; ACQUISITION; WORDS
AB Research over the past decade has identified gamification as an emerging approach in language teaching, particularly in updating vocabulary instruction through game-based methods. This study investigates the efficacy of Kahoot! in improving learners' recall and retention of ESP vocabulary within the Chinese EFL context. A total of 61 participants were recruited and randomly assigned to two intact cohorts: an experimental group (N = 31) receiving instruction through Kahoot!, and a control group (N = 30) following traditional teaching practice. The Vocabulary Knowledge Scale (VKS) was employed to measure lexical recall and retention across three testing phases: pre-, post-, and delayed post-testing. Additionally, a questionnaire was administered to explore learners' perceptions of Kahoot!. The findings indicate that integrating Kahoot! into vocabulary instruction enhances both the recall and retention of ESP vocabulary items and has an overall positive impact on affective factors related to vocabulary learning. Further research is required to establish the generalizability of these results across various contexts, diverse EFL populations, and a wider range of ESP genres.

TI Developing university competencies through learning and exchange of
   ideas. A multidisciplinary approach
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Competence; Socrative; communication; strategies
ID SOCIAL NETWORKS; STUDENTS; GAMIFICATION; EDUCATION; INSTRUCTION;
   STRATEGIES
AB This study evaluated an educational strategy based on Socrative and social networks to develop competencies in 103 university students of optics and economics. The objective was to analyze how this methodology affects the self-perception of skills such as communication, leadership and collaborative learning. A quantitative, cross-sectional approach was used with pretest and post-test questionnaires and Likert scale items on five key competencies. The results revealed an increase in motivation and leadership self-perception, highlighting positive interaction in social networks and teamwork. Factor analysis confirmed the coherence of the competency factors studied. However, limitations were identified in the stability of the groups due to frequent changes of spokesperson and lack of control in the dynamics. This strategy is innovative in stimulating collaborative learning, enhancing students' motivation by using social networks.

TI A BOPPPS-based micro-lecture teaching intervention for orthopaedic
   postgraduates in China
SO MEDICAL EDUCATION ONLINE
LA English
DT Article
DE Micro-lectures; BOPPPS; practical teaching; orthopaedics research;
   postgraduate education
ID MEDICAL-EDUCATION
AB Background Orthopaedics is a highly specialized discipline characterized by complex theoretical frameworks and extensive knowledge, posing challenges for postgraduate teaching. With the advancement of educational technology, micro-lectures have been increasingly adopted in medical education. However, their isolated use may be insufficient to sustain student engagement or ensure effective learning. The bridge-in, learning objective, pre-test, participatory learning, post-assessment, and summary (BOPPPS) teaching model, which emphasizes the closed-loop management of student participation and teaching feedback, may address these limitations. Objective This study aims to develop and evaluate a BOPPPS-based micro-lecture teaching system for orthopaedic postgraduates, to improve teaching quality, enhance students' clinical competencies, and provide evidence-based insights for teaching reform. Methods Forty-eight first-year surgical postgraduates in a residency program were randomly divided into experimental and control groups (n = 24 per group). The experimental group received micro-lecture instruction integrated within the BOPPPS framework, while the control group received ordinary teaching. Both groups were assessed using the Mini-Clinical Evaluation Exercise and Direct Observation of Procedural Skills after clinical training. A questionnaire survey was conducted to gauge students' satisfaction with the BOPPPS-based micro-lectures. Results The experimental group showed significantly better clinical diagnostic and treatment abilities than the control group across most domains (p < 0.007). No significant differences were identified in humanistic care (p = 0.015), providing detailed information and informed consent (p = 0.190), or communication skills with patients (p = 0.209) after Bonferroni correction. Performance in preoperative preparation reached the threshold of significance (p = 0.005). The experimental group outperformed the control group in eight other clinical practice indicators (p < 0.005).

TI Revivalism in Indian educational philosophy: reinterpreting ancient
   wisdom for modern times
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Educational revivalism; Indian philosophy; cultural heritage; modern
   education; holistic development; curriculum integration; Educational
   revivalism; Indian philosophy; cultural heritage; modern education;
   holistic development; curriculum integration
AB The issue of educational revivalism has served as a reaction to demands of globalization and the escalating presence of standardized cultural commodities in India. The article begins by placing revivalism in the historical context of Indian education, showing that there was an underlying foundation in the Gurukula system of education and Vedic-Upanishadic principles, all of which were indigenous to Indian education. The article will determine how the Indian knowledge systems and philosophies may come to be seriously considered in the design of the curriculum, and how a balance between conservative and progressive education policies may be achieved. The article employs a normative or philosophical approach and uses historical writings and current policies to determine the extent of the revivalism ideology application and applicability in India today. It is demonstrated how revivalism has developed into a cultural defence against charges of dogmatic, speculative, and exclusionary moral-religious education, by contending to challenge this criticism based on a combination of traditional and modern learning. The article goes on to discuss the relevance of the revivalism to Indian and international education systems and suggests specific policy recommendations to incorporate indigenous knowledge in a way that is compatible with international academic discourses. The article also points toward the need for empirical studies on the outcomes of revivalist-inspired curricula, as well as comparative analyses across different cultural and educational contexts.

TI The science of butterflies:science is more than a one-hour lesson in a
   3rd grade classroom!
SO SCIENCE ACTIVITIES-PROJECTS AND CURRICULUM IDEAS IN STEM CLASSROOMS
LA English
DT Article
DE Common core (english language arts and math); Next Generation Science
   Standards; life Science; curriculum; instructional strategies
AB Many educators view butterfly lessons as short, one-hour activities focused solely on the life cycle, arguing that they are not multipurposeful. Thus, a simple activity where children build a paper plate model of the stages may take place in an hour-long lesson. McTighe and Brown believe that the Next Generation Science Standards (NGSS) provide a framework that emphasizes teaching for deep understanding rather than covering a wide range of topics superficially. Specifically, the disciplinary core ideas, science and engineering practices, and crosscutting concepts represent three interconnected dimensions that support teachers in facilitating and students in developing deep conceptual understanding of science. The primary argument of this article is that achieving depth of learning in science requires sustained instructional time rather than brief, one-hour lessons. We've designed a multi-month lesson on butterflies that incorporates the 5E (Engage, Explore, Explain, Elaborate, and Evaluate) model of instruction. This unit directly aligns with 3-LS1-1 and 3-LS4-2 Next Generation Science Standards for grade 3.

   Alkhashil, Maha Ali
TI Enhancing reflective thinking through active learning: a study on
   eighth-level undergraduate students in the history program
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Reflective thinking; active learning; critical engagement; history
   curriculum; higher education
AB Reflective thinking skills are critical for students' academic and professional development, enabling them to analyze information, evaluate arguments, and propose meaningful solutions. Recent studies have noted ongoing challenges in fostering reflective thinking in higher education, particularly in humanities disciplines. To address these concerns, the present study examined the effectiveness of active learning strategies in enhancing reflective thinking skills among undergraduate history students. Specifically, it investigated the integration of strategies such as brainstorming, problem-solving, storytelling, role-playing, and think-pair-share into the teaching of the unit 'The Far East after World War II'. A quasi-experimental one-group design with pre- and post-tests was employed, involving a purposive sample of 29 eighth-level students enrolled in the History Program at Princess Nourah Bint Abdulrahman University. The reflective thinking test measured five dimensions: visual vision, detecting fallacies, drawing conclusions, providing convincing explanations, and suggesting solutions. Results showed statistically significant improvements across all dimensions, with large effect sizes. These findings suggest that active learning fosters higher-order thinking and critical engagement in history education. The study recommends embedding active learning strategies into history curricula, training faculty, and developing evidence-based teaching resources. Future research should examine the long-term impacts of these strategies and their adaptability across disciplines and learning contexts.

TI How generative AI may influence the nature and future of the teaching
   career: a case study of Gen Z foreign language teachers
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Generative AI; ChatGPT; Gen Z Teachers; Language Education; Teaching
   Career; AI-Mediated Education; Education
AB Recent advances in AI tools have triggered concerns about the future of teaching careers and their replacement with such advanced systems. Though there is a growing deluge of literature on the use of AI in Education, there seems to be a paucity of research on how different forms of AI, especially generative AI, may influence the teaching profession itself. The present study, by focusing on Gen Z teachers as the most probable group of teachers accustomed to living with and using advanced technologies, explores this group's use of generative AI and their voices and concerns about their job and its future. Semi-structured interviews were used to gather data, which were then analyzed using content analysis in MAXQDA. The results showed that the participants used ChatGPT for different purposes including generating content, meeting learners' needs, evaluation, idea generation, and enhancing their professional knowledge. Regarding their professional future, they mostly believed that human teachers can still be involved in setting up, managing, and controlling advanced teaching systems and that they can find ways to cope with the new changes.

TI Shaping induction support: how collegial networks address early career
   teachers' psychological needs during induction
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Self-determination theory; teacher induction support; novice teachers;
   qualitative research; well-being
ID MENTORING BEGINNING TEACHERS; SELF-DETERMINATION; WORKPLACE; BURNOUT;
   SATISFACTION; MOTIVATION; RESOURCES
AB High attrition rates among early career teachers (ECTs) remain a major global concern, emphasizing the need for strong and sustained support during the induction phase. Existing research on induction support has primarily focused on ECTs' own perspectives, overlooking those of supporting actors such as mentors, pedagogical supervisors, school leaders, and colleagues. To strengthen induction practices, it is essential to understand how these actors perceive their roles in providing formal and informal support and which contextual factors shape their actions. Drawing on Self-Determination Theory, this study explored how different actors support or hinder ECTs' basic psychological needs for autonomy, relatedness, and competence. Twelve individual semi-structured interviews with mentors, pedagogical supervisors, school leaders, and colleagues revealed that competence support was most frequently discussed, followed by relatedness and autonomy support. Mentors, school leaders, and pedagogical supervisors mainly emphasized competence and autonomy, whereas colleagues primarily focused on relatedness. Formal professional support was largely provided by school leaders and pedagogical supervisors, while mentors and colleagues offered informal emotional and social support. These findings provide insight into how various actors conceptualize their supportive roles, the tensions and contextual influences they encounter, and offer practical implications for schools and policymakers aiming to enhance ECTs' induction experiences.

TI Methodological framework for pre-service teachers to effectively
   cultivate a variety of moral skills and values in senior students
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Case study; Kazakhstan; MDII; moral development; principles; secondary
   school
ID SELF-KNOWLEDGE; SUBJECT
AB This study aimed to construct and evaluate a methodological framework that would allow pre-service teachers to effectively cultivate a variety of moral skills and values in students in Grades 10 and 11 in the Senior Level in Kazakhstan. A total of 72 third- and fourth-year pre-service social educators and educational psychologists enrolled in an elective course and module 'The Contemporary Moral Education in Kazakhstan', which consisted of pedagogical and practical components. The course curriculum covered three distinct approaches: teaching through narrative, problem-oriented teaching, and moral competence promotion. The content (i.e. cinematographic, religious, and educational materials) of the course was geared toward interpreting moral values through the prism of selected pedagogies. After taking the course, students were asked to implement one of the suggested approaches during their three-month practicums in schools. The research instrument of the study was the Moral Development Interview Inventory (MDII). The results revealed a significant improvement in the moral development level of children after the intervention (p = 0.002, at p <= 0.005). No statistically significant differences were observed between the approaches (p = 0.108, at p <= 0.005), indicating that neither was more effective than the other. The present finding may be useful to teachers, secondary and higher educational institutions, parents, instructional designers, and other stakeholders in the field of moral education.

TI The portrayal of environmental literacy in Chinese language textbooks
   for non-native speakers
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE CLT textbooks; environmental literacy; non-native learners; critical
   discourse analysis; visual image analysis; CLT textbooks; environmental
   literacy; non-native learners; critical discourse analysis; visual image
   analysis
AB The focus of this analysis is the portrayal of environmental issues in Chinese language textbooks designed for non-native speakers. Two popular textbook series were analyzed through content analysis, Critical Discourse Analysis (CDA) and Visual Image Analysis (VIA) to indicate the embeddedness of environmental literacy (EL) and ideological messages. An effective conveyance of environmental knowledge and encouragement of certain behaviors is evident in the textbooks; however, the results indicate a lack of emphasis on the affective component related to environmental topics. Commonly, environmental challenges are framed as global problems that necessitate international cooperation. In addition, China's depiction is consistently positive, referencing traditional environmental values, philosophies, and responsible practices. A tendency in the textbooks is the promotion of technological innovation as the primary solution for environmental issues. Such a perspective corresponds with state and corporate agendas. This alignment frequently results in overlooking public concerns and reducing opportunities for the development of critical environmental consciousness. Therefore, recommendation from this study is for textbook developers to incorporate the overlooked environmental issues into textbooks. In addition, it is recommended that educators incorporate supplementary resources such as news articles and multimedia content. This integration aims to offer students with more extensive perspectives and cultivate a critical assessment of environmental narratives and their omissions. The scope of this research is confined to Chinese Language Teaching (CLT) materials. It is therefore suggested that future research explore how other disciplines, including geography and general language education, approach environmental issues and ideologies.

TI Understanding perceived learning and the role of digital self-efficacy
   in generative AI adoption among STEM and non-STEM students
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Generative artificial intelligence; digital self-efficacy; technology
   acceptance model; perceived learning; STEM vs. non-STEM; higher
   education
ID TECHNOLOGY ACCEPTANCE MODEL; USER ACCEPTANCE; FIT INDEXES; SATISFACTION;
   INFORMATION; COLLEGE; EDUCATION
AB This study examined how Digital Self-Efficacy (DSE) shapes the adoption of generative artificial intelligence (AI) tools and their relationship with students' Perceived Learning in higher education. STEM refers to science, technology, engineering, and mathematics, while non-STEM includes disciplines such as the social sciences, humanities, and arts. Focusing on widely accessible generative AI applications for writing, coding, and information processing, the study integrates the Technology Acceptance Model (TAM) and Social Cognitive Theory (SCT). Survey data from 677 undergraduates in Ecuador were analyzed using Partial Least Squares Structural Equation Modeling and complemented with follow-up interviews. Results indicate that higher DSE increases perceived usefulness and ease of use of AI tools, which in turn foster positive attitudes and stronger intentions to adopt them. Behavioral intention to use AI is positively associated with perceived learning, with this effect being stronger among no-STEM students. Qualitative findings further show that STEM students primarily use AI fortechnical or task-specific work, whereas non-STEM students reported broader gains in digital confidence and efficiency. Overall, the study highlights the central role of digital self-efficacy in enabling meaningful adoption of generative AI in higher education, while acknowledging that findings are based on self-reported perceptions from a digitally active student population.

TI Governing education 'problems' in Botswana: what's the problem
   represented to be in the education & training sector Strategic Plan
   (2015-2020)
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Education policy; ETSSP; WPR: problematization; Botswana; Arts &
   Humanities; Humanities; Media & Film Studies; Journalism & Professional
   Media; Publishing; Arts; Music; Music & Education; Early Years; Social
   Sciences; Education
ID SAN; REFORM
AB This paper draws from Carol Bacchi's 'What's the Problem Represented to Be' (WPR), to examine how the Education and Training Sector Strategic Plan (2015-2020) represents education 'problems' in Botswana. Although there are widely held views that education problems exist in Botswana, there is lack of empirical and theory-driven studies that critically examine how such problems are represented as policy issues. It is largely unclear how some issues come to be represented as education 'problems', the politics of their formation, and the effects of such problematizations. Through WPR analysis, the study reveals two (2) problematizations; the mismatch between tertiary qualifications and labor market needs, and access to, and quality of education. This is what is assumed to be the problems of education in Botswana. The WPR analysis disentangles how these problems are represented as problems, the politics of their formation, their silences and the material effects they have on the education sector in Botswana. The paper concludes by discussing the implications of the WPR analysis and emphasize the need to unmake and re-think the existing education problem representations to allow for alternative conceptualizations.

TI Exploring new approaches to the development of career adaptability for
   future pedagogues and psychologists: testing a methodology based on
   group consultations
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Adaptation; career adapt-abilities scale; career adaptability; higher
   education; professional mobility; specialist training
ID CONSTRUCTION; STUDENTS
AB A fundamental skill for aspiring educational psychologists entering dynamic, complex job environments is professional mobility. This study created and assessed a twelve-session group consultation course aimed at enhancing career flexibility and preparing students for evolving professional demands. In two Kazakh institutions, entire courses of 150 undergraduate pedagogy and psychology students were randomized to either the intervention or the standard curriculum using a quasi-experimental pre-test/post-test approach. The Career Adapt-Abilities Scale, which measures concern, control, curiosity, and confidence, was used to gauge career adaptability. The results were contextualized using a motivation profile. Concern +7.29, Control +7.65, Curiosity +5.53, Confidence +4.90, and total adaptability +21.9 were among the significant gains (all p < 0.001) that the intervention produced compared to the control; post-test comparisons similarly favored the intervention.

TI Representation of Greek female authors in Greek language textbooks for
   Greek ethnic minority schools in Albania
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Modern Greek; Mother Tongue 6-9; Albania; language; education
AB The Greek ethnic minority in Albania maintains a distinct linguistic and cultural heritage, supported in part through education in the mother tongue. Language textbooks used in these schools are not only tools for teaching but also vehicles for transmitting cultural values, social norms, and perspectives. In this context, the representation of authors-particularly the balance between male and female voices-can influence students' perceptions of gender roles and equality. This study focuses on the presence of female writers of contemporary Greek literature in the language textbooks of the Greek minority in Albania, specifically in the series Mother Tongue for grades 6-9. Drawing on feminist literary theory, it examines how gender identity may shape both the production and reception of literary texts. The research employs quantitative and qualitative content analyses, with data presented in tables and pie charts to test the hypotheses. The findings reveal the range, type, and period of female authors included in the textbooks, which are then examined in the Discussion section. The results carry both practical and theoretical implications, offering insights into the role of female authors in Greek minority education and formulating proposals for enriching curricula with more diverse and equitable literary voices. IMPACT STATEMENTThis study provides the first systematic analysis of the representation of femaleauthors in Greek-language textbooks used in Greek ethnic minority schools in Albania.By combining quantitative and qualitative content analysis with feminist literary andeducational theory, the research highlights persistent gender imbalances in the selec-tion of literary texts, despite women's strong presence in textbook authoring teams.The findings demonstrate how limited female authorship may shape students'percep-tions of gender roles, cultural identity, and social values in minority education con-texts. The study has important implications for curriculum design, textbookdevelopment, and educational policy, particularly in minority and bilingual settings. Byoffering evidence-based recommendations for the inclusion of women's literary voices,it contributes to ongoing international debates on gender equality, multicultural edu-cation, and inclusive curricula. More broadly, the research supports efforts to aligneducational materials with UNESCO and European gender-mainstreaming goals, pro-moting equity, representation, and cultural sustainability in education.

TI Secondary teachers' perceptions on the introduction of the high school
   credit system
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE High school credit system; 2022 revised English national curriculum;
   teachers' perceptions; teachers' roles; South Korean national
   curriculum; curriculum reform; secondary English education; educational
   policy implementation
AB The introduction of the High School Credit System (HSCS) marks a milestone in secondary education in Korea, as it aligns with the 2022 Revised English National Curriculum (2022 RENC) and fosters a learner-centered approach and competency development in students. This study explores teachers' perceptions of the HSCS, focusing on its role in enhancing students' core competencies as stated in the 2022 RENC, and the anticipated challenges in its implementation. Data were collected from both middle and high school teachers through surveys and in-depth interviews. Descriptive statistics and independent t-tests revealed that teachers with more experience expressed greater skepticism toward the system as participants cited concerns over increased administrative workload, the need for additional training, and the pressure to teach outside their areas of expertise. Despite these drawbacks, many participants acknowledged benefits such as offering students greater autonomy and course flexibility, which could foster motivation and better align with future career aspirations. Though HSCS may offer opportunities for students to exercise autonomy and flexibility in choosing courses, participants noted that students may need structured guidance in course selection to better align with their career goals. This study highlights the advantage of HSCS in fostering autonomous learning, yet also points out the need for comprehensive teacher training and support for the successful implementation of HSCS in the field. The findings of this study contribute to the current discussions about educational reform in Korea. For HSCS to succeed, a proper balance should be found in preparing teachers and achieving the educational goals promoted by the 2022 RENC.

TI Data literacy in Chilean and Spanish teacher education: a
   cross-sectional study of self-efficacy, digital competencies, and
   anxiety
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Self-efficacy; digital competence; data use; data driven decision
   making; data literacy; teacher education; Self-efficacy; digital
   competence; data use; data driven decision making; data literacy;
   teacher education
ID DRIVEN DECISION-MAKING; VALIDATION
AB This study explored the perceptions of self-efficacy, anxiety, and perceived digital competencies specifically related to data use among pre-service teachers and university faculty in Chile and Spain. A cross-sectional survey design was employed using a validated instrument administered during the second semester of 2023 and the first semester of 2024. Data were collected from 606 participants (512 pre-service teachers and 94 university faculty) through online and in-person procedures and analyzed using non-parametric statistics due to non-normality. The results showed that Chilean faculty exhibited higher self-efficacy in the interpretation and application of data compared to their Spanish counterparts. Additionally, Chilean pre-service teachers demonstrated greater self-efficacy than their Spanish peers across all scales. Contextual and cultural differences between the countries may explain some of the observed variations. This study highlights the need for future research to examine how institutional pressure, curricular frameworks, and other contextual variables influence the perception and use of data in educational decision-making, which should be considered in teacher education programs.

TI Determinants of student burnout among undergraduate private university
   students in Bangladesh: a structural equation modeling
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Student burnout; self-evaluation trait; career decision making
   difficulties; social support; undergraduate students; Social Sciences;
   Behavioral Sciences; Cognitive Psychology; Reading, Psychology of;
   Dyslexia; Attention; Mental Health; Psychological Disorders; Learning
   Difficulties and Mental Retardation in Children; Language, Psychology
   of; Developmental Psychology; Perceptual Development; Bilingualism -
   Second Language; Cognitive Development; Language Development; School
   Psychology; Assessment and Testing
ID SOCIAL SUPPORT; ACADEMIC-PERFORMANCE; ENGAGEMENT; VARIANCE;
   CONSERVATION; PREDICTORS; RESOURCES
AB Student burnout is a growing concern among undergraduate students. This study investigates the influence of Self-Evaluation Traits (SET), Career Decision-Making Difficulties (CDMD), and Social Support (SS) on student burnout, guided by the Conservation of Resources (COR) theory. A cross-sectional research design was employed, with data collected through convenience sampling from 401 undergraduate students across ten private universities in Dhaka city, Bangladesh. Structural equation modeling was used to examine the hypothesized relationships. The findings reveal that both SET and CDMD significantly contribute to student burnout. Social Support also has a significant direct effect on burnout. However, the interaction between SET and Social Support does not significantly influence student burnout. Social Support partially mediates the relationship between CDMD and student burnout, highlighting its importance in alleviating burnout among students facing career decision challenges. No mediating effect of Social Support was found in the SET-burnout relationship. This study contributes to the limited literature on undergraduate student burnout in Bangladesh and underscores the importance of strengthening social support systems. The findings offer valuable insights for academic policymakers and educational institutions seeking to reduce burnout and improve student well-being.

TI Generating multiple-choice questions using reverse engineering
   techniques
SO MEDICAL EDUCATION ONLINE
LA English
DT Article
DE Educational measurement; multiple-choice questions; generative
   artificial intelligence; educational technology; nursing education
AB The study is aimed at exploring the potential of using ChatGPT-4 to develop multiple-choice questions that adhere to item development principles through the application of reverse engineering techniques in nursing education. To determine whether ChatGPT-4 could generate multiple-choice questions regarding medical-surgical nursing, the researchers first evaluated ChatGPT-4's level of nursing knowledge. The researchers then developed 20 prompts using reverse engineering techniques; these were subsequently entered into ChatGPT-4, resulting in the generation of 60 multiple-choice questions. All 60 questions were reviewed by five nursing education experts to evaluate compliance with item development principles. ChatGPT-4 demonstrated a high accuracy rate (98.00%) when answering multiple-choice questions about medical-surgical nursing education. AI-generated questions primarily adhered to the principles of item development, with an average compliance score of 7.90/9.00. The least adhered to among the nine-question development principles was 'The question stem should use only essential words or phrases.' Given that the AI-generated questions may serve as preliminary drafts for use in nursing education following expert review, ChatGPT-4 can function as a valuable tool in the design of educational materials. When utilizing ChatGPT-4, it is beneficial for nursing educators to apply reverse engineering techniques that enhance question quality through repeated comparison, as well as to analyze the prompts and corresponding AI-generated questions. Furthermore, including structured outlines and representative sample multiple-choice questions using Markdown syntax in the prompt can enhance the quality of AI-generated questions.

TI Exploring truth-seeking characteristics based on logical reasoning in

SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article

   problem solving; critical thinking disposition
ID CRITICAL THINKING DISPOSITION; NURSING-STUDENTS; TEACHERS
AB Truth-seeking is a critical thinking disposition that plays an essential role in solving mathematical literacy problems. However, many elementary school students lack truth-seeking skills. Therefore, action is needed to encourage students to develop truth-seeking skills. This study aims to describe the truth-seeking characteristics of elementary school students based on logical reasoning in solving mathematical literacy problems. The characteristics of truth-seeking observed include: (1) information analysis; (2) curiosity & questioning; (3) strategy development; (4) evaluation & verification; and (5) conclusion & reflection.

TI Decentralized education in Somalia: lessons from East African models
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Decentralized education; educational governance; East Africa; fiscal
   decentralization; community participation; local empowerment;
   Post-Conflict education; Decentralized education; educational
   governance; East Africa; fiscal decentralization; community
   participation; local empowerment; Post-Conflict education
ID TANZANIA
AB This study analyzes decentralized education reforms in Kenya, Uganda, and Tanzania and derives actionable lessons for Somalia's evolving federal system. Adopting a conceptual, comparative, and narrative review of peer-reviewed literature, government documents, and policy reports, the analysis is organized around administrative, fiscal, and political dimensions of decentralization. Evidence from East Africa indicates that devolution expands access and strengthens accountability when subnational authorities receive clear mandates, predictable intergovernmental transfers, and institutionalized avenues for community participation. However, capacity asymmetries, politicized decision-making, and weak oversight can entrench spatial inequities and erode quality. Somalia's context, fragmented federalism, donor dependence, irregular fiscal flows, and a dominant private sector amid low enrolment, amplifies these risks. The study proposes four priority reforms: transparent, formula-based fiscal equalization to promote equity, clarified federal-state roles to reduce duplication and improve implementation, legally empowered school- and community-level accountability mechanisms, and integration of private providers through standards, routine data reporting, and performance-linked regulation. It further argues that digital public infrastructure can bolster monitoring, financial transparency, and learning assessment where administrative capacity is thin. The paper contributes to education governance in fragile settings by translating regional experience into a sequenced, context-sensitive reform agenda for Somalia.

TI Middle school students' experience of joy in different activities during
   physical education-a case study
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Enjoyment; activity; students; movement; physical education
ID INTRINSIC MOTIVATION
AB Joy of movement is central in the physical education subject and has received more attention in the public debate and in research following the new Norwegian curriculum in 2020. There is a lack of quantitative studies that measure students joy of movement in activities in the subject-which this study addresses regarding the activities of dance, strength/endurance, play, football, and ball sport. Data collection was conducted using a questionnaire among 269 secondary school students in Mid-Norway. The results show that secondary school students generally experience joy of movement in most activities. Furthermore, the study shows that boys generally experience a higher degree of joy of movement than girls in all activities, except for dance, where girls report higher numbers. Ball sports stand out positively in terms of the experience of joy of movement, while the findings indicate a need for change in the teaching of dance. The findings of this study related to dance suggest that current dance teaching does not create joy of movement among boys and only limited joy of movement among girls. To achieve a change, there will be a need to increase teachers' competence in dance in general, and in dance from youth culture in particular.

TI Exploring student volunteer experiences through the lens of procedural
   and relational switching costs
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Disability; switching cost; university students; volunteering;
   qualitative research
AB This study explores the experiences of student volunteers by examining procedural and relational switching costs, as reflected in their perceptions of the benefits and challenges associated with participating in data collection for a large-scale sports disability research project. A qualitative research design was employed, and semi-structured in-person interviews were conducted with 20 student volunteers to capture their experiences. Thematic analysis revealed that 'procedural switching costs stemmed from scheduling conflicts, technical difficulties, and language barriers, while relational switching costs emerged through challenges in building rapport with interview participants'. Despite these obstacles, volunteers reported gaining valuable skills and meaningful experiences through their participation. The findings underscore the importance of addressing switching costs to enhance volunteer experiences and project effectiveness. Practical recommendations include providing targeted training in communication and trust-building, forming diverse research teams, offering consistent faculty support, and developing flexible research protocols. Additionally, ensuring clear communication procedures, technological support, and access to translators were identified as key strategies to streamline the research process. By implementing these measures, institutions and researchers can create a more supportive environment for student volunteers, thereby improving engagement and outcomes in disability sports research. This study contributes to a deeper understanding of volunteer dynamics and offers actionable strategies to support student involvement in research with marginalised communities.

TI A model for promoting entrepreneurial skills in higher education:
   evidence from Jordan
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Entrepreneurial skills; entrepreneurship education; entrepreneurship and
   innovation; higher education; model development; Education
ID UNIVERSITY-STUDENTS; COMPETENCES; INTENTIONS; IMPACT
AB This study examined a model for nurturing entrepreneurial skills within higher education at the University of Jordan. The study focused on entrepreneurship education dimensions includes: educational content, teaching methods, and educational learning environment. Using a descriptive-analytical study design, supplemented with a quantitative survey, data were collected using a convenience sample method from 384 students enrolled in an entrepreneurship and innovation course in the academic year 2023/2024. Multiple regression analysis was utilized to better understand the impact of the various dimensions on nurturing entrepreneurial skills. The study determined that the entrepreneurship education program does foster entrepreneurial competencies among students. The data demonstrated that the educational content was the most important factor, as it provided the relevant knowledge and practical skills needed to execute entrepreneurial ideas. Teaching methods also had positive effects on entrepreneurial competencies and employed active learning forms to enhance cooperative interaction with fellow students. The learning environment, characterized by positive motivational dimensions and resources, provides the creative socialization needed to foster new ideas. The findings affirm the value of correcting a proper balance between applying quality content, delivering engaging pedagogy, and utilizing a conducive environment to maximize key learning outcomes from entrepreneurship educational practice.

TI Barriers to developing effective ESP teaching materials at Gorontalo
   State University
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Learning failure; English for specific purposes (ESP); state university;
   teaching materials; ESP instructors; higher education; Arts &
   Humanities; Arts; Media Communication; Humanities; Cultural Studies;
   Media & Film Studies; Media & Communications; Communication Studies
ID ENGLISH; PURPOSES
AB Identifying the reasons behind the unsuccessful development of English for specific purposes (ESP) teaching materials in Indonesian state universities is crucial to enhancing educational quality and better preparing students for professional demands. This study aims to systematically map the key factors contributing to these failures. Employing a qualitative descriptive approach, the study identifies four primary areas affecting the development of ESP teaching materials. The first area - material availability - encompasses essential resources such as syllabi, semester lesson plans (RPS) and instructional content. Second, lecturer aspects involve the preparedness and capability of ESP instructors in designing and delivering relevant content. Third, structural factors include institutional support for lecturers during the material development process. Finally, infrastructural elements relate to facilities and access to necessary resources. This study advocates for further comprehensive research to optimize the development of ESP teaching materials and ensure alignment with students' academic goals and the competencies required in their respective professional fields.

TI Roles of school administrative support supervision on teachers'
   competence in the implementation of competency-based curriculum in Lango
   secondary schools, Uganda
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE School administrators; support supervision; teachers 'competence;
   competency based curriculum; Lango subregion; Northern Uganda; Social
   Sciences; Education; Classroom Practice; Assessment & Testing; Teaching
   & Learning
ID LEADERSHIP
AB This study examined the influence of school administrative support supervision on teachers' competence in implementing the Competency-Based Curriculum (CBC) in secondary schools in Uganda's Lango sub-region. Grounded in Goal Path Theory and Gross's Theory of Curriculum Implementation, the study employed a quantitative correlational research design to determine the relationship between administrative supervision and teachers' competence. A sample of thirty-seven secondary schools, comprising public and private institutions, was selected using stratified random sampling techniques. Data were collected using a structured questionnaire based on a five-point Likert scale and analysed using SPSS version 27. The results revealed a strong, positive, and statistically significant relationship between school administrative support supervision and teachers' competence in CBC implementation (r = .857, p < .001). Regression analysis indicated that improvements in administrative supervision significantly predicted increases in teachers' competence, with the model explaining 73.4 percent of the variance in competence levels (R-2 = .734). The study contributes novel empirical evidence from a developing-country context by demonstrating that administrative supervision functions not only as an evaluative mechanism but also as a transformative tool that enhances teacher competence and supports effective CBC implementation. It emphasizes sustained, structured supervision as a critical lever for improving curriculum implementation outcomes nationally.

TI Academic engagement and autonomous motivation: a study in South
   Ethiopia, secondary schools
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Academic engagement; autonomous motivation; controlled motivation;
   secondary school; student motivation; Social Sciences; Behavioral
   Sciences; Cognitive Psychology; Reading, Psychology of Behavioral
   Sciences; Dyslexia; Attention; Mental Health; Psychological Disorders;
   Learning Difficulties & Mental Retardation in Children; Language,
   Psychology of Social Sciences; Developmental Psychology; Perceptual
   Development; Bilingualism - Second Language; Cognitive Development;
   Language Development; School Psychology; Assessment & Testing
ID STUDENTS MOTIVATION; SUPPORT; ASSOCIATION; PROFILES; PRESSURE; QUALITY
AB Alarming 94.6% of Ethiopian students failed their essential secondary school examination, highlighting a crisis in the education system that requires an examination of students' autonomous motivation. Numerous studies have focused on predicting levels of engagement through motivation, which has been overlooked. However, research on the impact of academic engagement on autonomous motivation is lacking. This study investigated the correlation between academic engagement and autonomous motivation among secondary school students in the south region state, Ethiopia. The researchers administered self-report surveys to 383 students who participated through random selection. Descriptive statistics and multiple regression models were used to analyze the relationships. Most of the students showed controlled motivational patterns. However, academic engagement was related to both forms of motivation. Female students showed higher levels of controlled and autonomous motivation than male students. These findings indicate that disregarding students' interests and perspectives diminishes autonomous motivation. Curriculum design should encourage students to be self-motivated using assessment systems and feedback loops, ensuring that indicators of student development and motivation are aligned, and using teaching methods that have different learning styles for males and females.

TI A mixed-methods investigation of critical friend groups as a tool for
   honing teachers' practice
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Critical friend groups; community of practice; reflective practice; EFL
   teachers' reflection; peer reflection; critical friends
AB This mixed-methods study investigated the effectiveness of Critical Friend Groups (CFGs) in improving EFL teachers' practices. A sequential explanatory design was adopted. Ten CFGs were formed, each comprising two novice teachers and one experienced teacher (critical friend). Over a 10-week period, novice teachers observed each other's classes using a researcher-developed checklist and engaged in weekly CFG discussions facilitated by the experienced teacher. Quantitative data were collected through classroom observation checklists and analyzed using paired-sample t-tests and effect size calculations to examine changes in teaching performance. Qualitative data were obtained from transcribed CFG sessions and analyzed using inductive content analysis to extract themes reflecting teachers' reflective development. Quantitative findings revealed significant improvement in teaching scores over time, particularly in the early weeks. Qualitative analysis identified themes across three stages of teaching: Before the stage (e.g. lesson planning, dealing with heterogeneity), On the stage (e.g. reflection, classroom management), and After the stage (e.g. assessment challenges, emotional labor, recognition). Overall, the findings demonstrate that CFGs can serve as effective, context-sensitive tools for enhancing teacher reflection and professional growth, especially in under-researched EFL settings.

TI Understanding mathematics anxiety: the role of social-emotional skills
   and school support
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Mathematics anxiety; social and emotional skills; gender differences;
   perceived teacher support; perceived lesson quality; Arts & Humanities;
   Humanities; Media & Film Studies; Journalism & Professional Media;
   Publishing; Arts; Music; Music & Education; Early Years; Social
   Sciences; Education
ID SOCIOECONOMIC-STATUS; ACHIEVEMENT EMOTIONS; ACADEMIC-ACHIEVEMENT;
   5-FACTOR MODEL; METAANALYSIS; PERSONALITY; GENETICS; GRIT
AB Mathematics anxiety negatively affects students' learning and well-being, but the role of social and emotional skills has received less attention. This study examines how mathematics anxiety is associated with social and emotional skills (curiosity, perseverance, emotional control, stress resistance, assertiveness, empathy, and co-operation) and school-related factors (perceived teacher support and mathematics lesson quality) along with demographic predictors such as gender and socio-economic status. Data from 6,392 15-year-old students from the PISA 2022 Estonian sample were analysed using hierarchical regression. Higher stress resistance (beta = -0.17), perseverance (beta = -0.15), emotional control (beta = -0.12), and lower empathy (beta = 0.09) predict lower mathematics anxiety, underscoring the role of social and emotional learning (SEL). Perceived lesson quality (beta = -0.29), teacher support (beta = -0.07) and students' socio-economic status (beta = -0.06) are also significant predictors for lower mathematics anxiety. Interactions between gender and perseverance, stress resistance, and lesson quality, as well as between socioeconomic status and perseverance, were also statistically significant in the prediction model. These findings suggest that improving students' social-emotional skills and lesson quality could reduce anxiety and foster a more supportive learning environment. The study provides new insights for educators and policymakers in addressing mathematics anxiety.

TI Hong Kong primary teachers' perceptions and attitudes towards the
   infusion of values education in English Language teaching
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Cross-curricular learning (CCL); English Language Teaching; integrated
   approach; integrated curriculum; values education; teacher agency;
   whole-person development
ID BELIEFS
AB Cross-curricular learning (CCL) seeks to integrate knowledge and skills across different subjects, thereby promoting cohesive and holistic learning experiences for students. This small-scale study explores the perceptions and attitudes of Hong Kong primary school teachers in regard to embedding values education into English teaching and evaluates the effectiveness of such an integrative approach. Semi-structured interviews were conducted with three frontline teachers. The findings reveal mixed feelings experienced by the teachers towards the approach. Although the importance of values education was recognized, feelings of confusion and stress were also expressed, indicating the possible tension between teacher agency and the policy imposed on the integrated curriculum. Further, the results indicate that they encountered three major practical challenges in infusing values education in English teaching: limited class time for covering both content and values, the absence of a consistent evaluation system for assessing values education and inadequate institutional or administrative support. This study highlights important implications for the feasibility of embedding values in the teaching of English and shed light on the future development of values education and integrated approach across subjects.

TI An investigation of strategies adopted to prevent violence in schools in
   Saudi Arabia: educators' and parents' perspectives
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE School violence; prevention strategies; teachers' perceptions; parents'
   perceptions
ID SAFETY; PERCEPTIONS; STUDENT
AB The issue of school violence is a pressing societal issue. In response to escalating worries regarding school safety and heightened public scrutiny, educational authorities are actively taking measures and putting in place strategies aimed at mitigating incidents of school violence. However, these strategies work best when schools, parents and the society are involved. This study investigates the strategies employed by schools to prevent violence, with a particular focus on the perceptions of teachers and parents. Surveys were administered to both groups to gather their views on the effectiveness of these strategies, and the results were analysed using Bronfenbrenner's ecological theory. The methodology involved a quantitative research design with a structured survey, enabling a systematic exploration of the prevalent forms of violence and the strategies used in response. The key findings reveal that early intervention in disruptive behaviour, monitoring students outside classrooms, organising crisis response teams, and encouraging parental involvement are among the most employed strategies. Notably, this research fills a gap in existing literature, offering the first empirical investigation into school violence prevention within Saudi Arabia, a context where limited studies have previously been conducted. This research contributes valuable insights to the academic understanding of school safety in Saudi schools.

TI Reimagining the unthinkable: Transnational queer students' critical
   duoethnographic journeys of linguistic empowerment
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Counternarrative writings; critical duoethnography; heteronormative
   literacy practices; transnational queer students; queer and gendered
   literacies; Counternarrative writings; critical duoethnography;
   heteronormative literacy practices; transnational queer students; queer
   and gendered literacies
AB Increasing evidence indicates that scholarship in applied linguistics and language and literacy education often centers on heteronormative frameworks that constrain how identities are constructed and negotiated. Immigrants and transnational teachers, however, have increasingly problematized monolingualism, predetermined identities, and bounded geographies through acts of self-transformation. Despite learners crossing geographical and cultural boundaries, research on transnational queer students (TQSs) remains underdeveloped, creating space to theorize queer and gendered literacies, denaturalize 'heteronormative literacy practices', and resist oppressed identities in U.S. heteronormative contexts. This study illuminates how two TQSs' linguistic repertoires are portrayed and reimagined within such frameworks. Drawing on critical duoethnography and counternarrative writings, this study aims to unsettle heteronormative paradigms through gendered and racialized identity formations. This study concludes with a discussion of limitations, implications for language and literacy educators, and questions arising from this work.

TI Towards recognizing the heterogeneity of English medium instruction
   (EMI) teacher backgrounds
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE English medium instruction; EMI teachers; L2 English proficiency;
   teacher development; teacher background; teacher identity; English
   medium instruction; EMI teachers; L2 English proficiency; teacher
   development; teacher background; teacher identity
ID MEDIUM EDUCATION; LANGUAGE; UNIVERSITIES; LECTURERS
AB As the number of English medium instruction (EMI) courses around the world continues to increase, so too does the number of teachers operating in EMI. Previous research on EMI teachers has investigated teacher identity construction, teacher development, perspectives on the role of language in teaching and learning, and classroom practices. However, much existing literature on EMI teachers tends to treat them as a homogeneous group and overlooks the heterogeneity that may offer additional and more nuanced insights into teachers working on the frontlines of EMI. To address this gap, the present study emphasizes the heterogeneity of EMI teachers by investigating two groups of teachers working in the Swedish EMI context: Swedish EMI teachers and expatriate EMI teachers in Sweden. Employing qualitative semi-structured interviews analyzed through deductive content analysis, it examines between-group variables such as whether teachers share a first language with students and the amount and location(s) of their EMI teaching experience. The interviews covered the following topics: teacher background and development; views on student group characteristics; and perspectives on student English proficiency. Comparisons between the Swedish teachers and the expatriate teachers in Sweden show several overlaps but also divergences, including the impact of having EMI experience from multiple contexts and the option of translanguaging.

TI Translation, adaptation, and validation of the Malay Preschool Screening
   Instrument for Targeting Educational Risk (MyPra-SIFTER): a
   teacher-administered hearing screening tool for preschool children in
   Malaysia
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Screening instrument; hearing loss; questionnaire; preschool students;
   preschool teachers
ID LANGUAGE; NOISE
AB Early detection of hearing loss is essential to minimize academic and social delays in preschoolers. In Malaysia, the number of teacher-administered screening tools is limited. This study aimed to translate, adapt and validate the Preschool Screening Instrument for Targeting Educational Risk (Preschool SIFTER) into Malay for use by teachers. Face validity was assessed by 10 preschool teachers, with all items achieving >= 90% agreement. A total of 109 children aged 3-6 years were evaluated by 21 teachers using the Malay Preschool SIFTER (MyPra-SIFTER). Internal consistency was strong (Cronbach's alpha = 0.95), and test-retest reliability, assessed with 23 students, was high (ICC = 0.95). Criterion validity was examined by comparing communication and social behavior classifications ('Pass' or 'At-risk') with DPOAE screening outcomes ('Pass' or 'Refer') for 78 children. Both the communication and social behavior domains demonstrated a sensitivity of 33.3%, with specificity of 98.2% and 96.5%, respectively. Significant differences in mean domain scores were found between DPOAE pass and refer groups (p < 0.05). The MyPra-SIFTER demonstrated strong reliability and high specificity, indicating its usefulness in identifying children unlikely to have hearing-related difficulties, while its lower sensitivity highlights the need for use alongside audiological screening.
C1 [Abdul Rahim, Siti Khadijah; Mazlan, Rafidah; Abdul Aziz, Nadiah; Ishak, Wan Syafira; Mohd Yusoff, Wan Nur Hanim; Kar, Quar Tian] Univ Kebangsaan Malaysia UKM, Fac Hlth Sci, Audiol Programme, Kuala Lumpur, Malaysia.

TI The influences of gender on employers' understanding of graduates'
   employability and recruitment choices in China
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Culture of choice; employers; gender; graduate employability;
   institutional context
ID DISCRIMINATION; INEQUALITY; EDUCATION; CAREER; ROLES
AB Unequal graduate labour market outcomes for female graduates evidence their experiences of disadvantage in various forms, but our understanding of gender's intersection with graduate employability is limited, especially from employers' perspectives. This paper reports on a qualitative research project in which semi-structrued intervews were conducted with employers from different industries based in selected Chinese cities. These interviews probed their perceptions of graduate employability and their consequent recruitment choices in relation to gender. Findings suggest gender has considerable influence on employee recruitment, and male preference is clearly evident. Combining a cultural theory of choice and an institutional theory as a theoretical framework, the study provides a nuanced account of gender-influenced recruitment choice arising from an interplay of employers' individual preferences with cultural influences at multiply levels. Whilst national and traditional gender norms and stereotypes still greatly influence employers' understandings of graduate employability and perpetuate gender inequality in hiring practices, institutional culture and context also exert important mediating effects to such impacts. The study contributes significantly to the development of a more valid account of social behaviour - exemplified here in employers' recruitment practices - than that offered by rational choice-inspired theory that has influenced 'modernisation' accounts of contemporary life.

TI Encapsulation of the defined integral mathematical object of university
   students: a view from APOS theory
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Comprehension; genetic decomposition; definite integral; university
   education; and APOS theory; Comprehension; genetic decomposition;
   definite integral; university education; and APOS theory
AB Understanding the concept of definite integrals is a persistent challenge in university mathematics education, largely attributable to traditional teaching practices that prioritise mechanical procedures over conceptual depth. Many students experience a disconnect between the algebraic, geometric, and conceptual representations of the definite integral, leading to a fragmented understanding of the concept. Therefore, this study seeks an alternative to the difficulties students face in understanding the definite integral from a conceptual perspective. To address this issue, the article aims to strengthen students' understanding of the definite integral in the Bachelor of Mathematics programme at the University of Quind & iacute;o, using APOS theory. This theory conceives mathematical understanding as a progressive construction of mental schemas, facilitated by cognitive mechanisms such as internalisation, coordination, and encapsulation. The study is framed within a qualitative methodology and a hermeneutic-interpretive approach. Didactic sequences, problem-solving analysis, and focus groups were used as research instruments. The findings indicate that an approach focused on students' conceptual development, supported by appropriate teacher intervention, promotes deeper and more lasting learning. Consequently, teaching planning that incorporates genetic decomposition, contextualised activities, classroom reflection, and guided exercises improves understanding of the definite integral and supports connections among different representations.

TI 'Imagine and Make' pedagogy: impacts of combining Entrepreneurial
   Thinking, Design Thinking and 3D Printing technology on the learning
   experience in general education
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Entrepreneurial Thinking; Design Thinking; 3D Printing; project-based
   learning; general education; higher education; Study Skills; Curriculum
   Studies; Higher Education; Technology in Education
ID SELF-EFFICACY; STUDENTS; SKILLS
AB Entrepreneurial Thinking, Design Thinking, and 3D Printing are traditionally applied separately in specialized curricula but are increasingly integrated to enrich learning and foster essential skills. This paper examines the impact of the 'Imagine and Make' pedagogy, which combines these approaches within the project-based course Innovation, Leadership & Civic Engagement for undergraduate students from diverse disciplines at Qatar University. A structured survey was conducted with 152 of 175 students using pretest-posttest self-efficacy and knowledge gain assessments, complemented by posttest satisfaction surveys, and student performance on project reports and prototyping was evaluated. Results show significant improvements in student self-efficacy and equitable skills development across diverse backgrounds. Notable gains were observed in Design Thinking, particularly in managing ambiguity and prototyping, as well as in Entrepreneurial Thinking skills such as opportunity identification and creative problem-solving. Substantial improvements in 3D Printing skills highlight the value of hands-on learning, while knowledge gains were especially evident in mastering the 6Is of Innovation framework. Despite challenges in identifying social issues, results demonstrate the approach's potential to foster creativity, risk-taking and collaboration, while bridging interdisciplinary learning and equipping students with real-world problem-solving skills. This research underscores the value of integrating these methodologies in general education to create skill-enhancing learning environments.

   Fylkesnes, Sandra
TI Activating prejudice awareness in teacher education to foster critical
   reflections: an analysis of a student-centred assignment
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Prejudice awareness; critical thinking; pedagogical decision; social
   justice; teacher education
AB In this article, we argue that raising preservice teachers' awareness of their own prejudices may serve as an initial step towards more critically informed and socially just pedagogical decisions in future classrooms. We explore how a student-centred assignment in teacher education can activate implicit prejudice against marginalised groups. Data was collected from 32 groups across three teacher education programmes. In the assignment, preservice teachers were asked to rank potential tenants for a basement apartment. Each tenant was described using ambiguous identity markers, often associated with housing discrimination. The aim was to trigger implicit prejudice and ultimately reduce it. Using critical theory for social justice and theories of prejudice, our analysis shows that none of the identity markers led to positive associations. However, some markers (substance abuse, mental health issues, Nazi affiliation, Roma identity, youth and families with small children) triggered negative prejudice. Preservice teachers moreover highlighted the limited information provided, feelings of trust, safety, and control and morality as factors influencing their ranking. We conclude that the preservice teachers, through the assignment, developed an awareness of their prejudice towards vulnerable groups in society.

TI The impact of work-family conflict on teachers' turnover intention: the
   mediating role of emotional labor
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Teachers in higher vocational colleges; work-family conflict; teachers'
   turnover intention; emotional labor; social exchange theory; Asian
   Education; Educational Psychology; Management Education; Higher
   Education Management
ID EMPLOYEE TURNOVER; LEADERSHIP; BURNOUT; NURSES
AB This study aims to investigate the relationship between emotional labor and the impact of work-family conflict (WFC) on teachers' intention to resign. A survey was conducted among 420 teachers from higher vocational colleges in 11 cities in Jiangsu Province, China. Reliability and validity tests, along with structural equation modeling, indicate that WFC positively affects teachers' turnover intention. Moreover, emotional labor partially mediates the relationship between WFC and turnover intention. This study enriches the understanding of the relationship between WFC and turnover intention, highlights the mediating role of emotional labor, further expands the applicability of emotional labor theory, provides a theoretical basis for subsequent research and offers substantive teacher management suggestions for school managers. By reducing WFCs and optimizing teacher emotional management, the turnover rate of teachers can be reduced.
