TI New media literacy of communication faculty students in Kyrgyzstan: an
   investigation in the context of demographic variables
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Media literacy; university students; critical thinking; media
   consumption
ID FAKE NEWS
AB This study aims to determine the new media literacy levels of communication faculty students in Kyrgyzstan and to examine the relationship between these levels and demographic variables such as age, gender, class and internet usage time. The research was conducted using a cross-sectional survey model, and the data were obtained from 418 students studying at three state universities between September and December 2024. A new media literacy scale with a validity and reliability study was used as a data collection tool. The data obtained were analysed using frequency, t-test, ANOVA and Pearson correlation analysis in SPSS software. The findings show that students' new media literacy levels, in general, are above the midpoint of the scale, especially in the functional production and critical consumption sub-dimensions. Female students' media literacy levels were significantly higher than male students. It was also found that media literacy skills increased as age and grade level increased. However, no significant relationship was found between internet usage time and media literacy. In line with the findings, media literacy education should focus not only on technical skills but also on critical thinking, ethical awareness and productive participation.

TI Travel distance, transportation costs and students' evaluation of
   supported teaching in school within a college of education in Ghana
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Field experience; classroom observation; partner schools;
   macro-teaching; student teachers; bachelor of education
AB Supported Teaching in School (STS) was instituted to give student teachers the opportunity to develop their competence in the classroom. This article analyses the travel distance, transportation costs, and students' evaluation of the role of stakeholders in the STS. Using questionnaires and focus group discussions, data was collected from 282 students of the Seventh-day Adventist College of Education, Agona-Ashanti. A stratified random sampling procedure was adopted. Frequency tables and inferential statistics in IBM SPSS (version 25) and Microsoft Word were used to analyse quantitative data. Thematic network analysis was used to analyse qualitative data. The results show that the average weekly distance travelled by students from the college to the partner schools from level 100 to level 300 was 4.734 km; thus, 9.468 km per week for a return journey. The frequency at which students engaged in STS in nearby schools was higher compared to the remote schools. Students' overall rating of the college in the organisation of the STS did not significantly differ with reference to gender (p > .05). Spending on a trip for STS was a financial restraint. The provision of means of transportation to the colleges of education is critical to easing the burden of transportation on students.

TI The challenges facing higher education in Puntland in research and
   academic publishing: suggestions for their improvement
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Research barriers; higher education institutions (HEIs); Puntland;
   capacity development; government support; quality of higher education
AB Academic research is a core component of the role of higher education institutions (HEIs) in generating knowledge, contributing to national development, and enhancing institutional prestige. Though higher education in Puntland is relatively young, as it appeared after the civil war and collapse of the Somali Central Government, the research output of Puntland HEIs is considerably low. The present study aims to identify the challenges that hinder research and academic publishing in Puntland HEIs. Using a questionnaire, data were collected from 90 academic staff of Puntland HEIs in the period of 11-24 November 2024, utilizing a convenience sampling method. . The study found that barriers to research and academic publishing in Puntland HEIs included a lack of funding, absence of promotion linked to publications and incentives, inadequate research infrastructures, insufficient time, knowledge, and skills, limited experience in publication procedures, a poor research-oriented culture, and restricted access to literature. The author recommends enhancing academic staff capabilities by offering scholarships, training, and mentorship, reforming institutional policies, implementing reward and promotion schemes, engaging the Puntland Diaspora, collaborating with international researchers to access scientific databases and providing governmental funding to HEIs to improve the research output in Puntland HEIs.
C1 [Jama, Saaid Said] Univ Hlth Sci, Fac Med, Bossaso, Puntland, Somalia.

TI Analyzing student engagement: a case study of active learning in two
   biology courses from a three-point perspective
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Student-centered; instructional approaches; active learning; student
   engagement; triangulation method; Social Sciences; Education; Higher
   Education; Study of Higher Education; Teaching & Learning
ID THINKING; SCIENCE
AB Active learning is being encouraged in higher education as alternatives to a lecture-based approach. This study investigates the impact of active learning on student engagement across the three-dimensional construct (emotional, behavioral, cognitive). A mixed-methods design was employed with data drawn from four cohorts of undergraduate biology students (n = 131) from two biology courses at a French university that use active learning as an instructional approach. Engagement was analyzed through a three-point perspective: non-participant observation (timeline analysis), teacher interviews, and student questionnaires including open-ended questions. Results showed that students reported higher motivation and collaboration; the teachers expressed satisfaction with the instructional approach and the level of interaction during the sessions; and the timeline analysis revealed sustained engagement throughout class sessions. Findings indicated that triangulating these perspectives provided valuable insights into how active learning fosters student engagement. Future research should investigate the use of different measuring tools when it comes to active learning approaches to teaching at the university level.

TI Beyond the classroom: a qualitative study of voluntary home visits to
   older adults as a tool for empathy and professional growth in medical
   students

LA English
DT Article
DE Geriatrics; first year students; student-patient interaction;
   volunteering; qualitative research; reflective writing
ID DOCTORS ATTITUDES
AB Global populations are rapidly aging, posing significant challenges. Yet, ageism among clinicians and medical students persists, undermining empathy and care quality. Traditional short-term educational efforts have limited effect; sustained, relationship-based programs hold promise but remain understudied. To address this, we implemented a year-long service-learning project, where first-year medical students visit elderly individuals at their homes to engage in light physical activities and discuss life challenges. This qualitative study examines the project's impact on first, second, and third-year medical students.From August to October 2020, we recruited, via convenience sampling, first-year medical students of three consecutive cohorts (n = 313) that completed ten home visits with older adults; of these sixty (19%) students participated in focus groups/interviews and 128 (41%) submitted reflective assignments. Data was manually analyzed using Braun and Clarke's six-phase reflexive thematic approach until saturation. We adhered to the COREQ (Consolidated Criteria for Reporting Qualitative Research) checklist.Three major themes and seven sub-themes emerged. First, building trusting patient-physician relationships, characterized by empathy, trust, and effective communication, alongside a balance between physician responsibility and patient autonomy. Second, embracing uncertainty and holistic care, which involved navigating medical ambiguity and integrating psychosocial dimensions into clinical reasoning. Third, reflecting on vulnerability, aging, and mortality, encompassing the emotional impact of disability and decline; reframing aging positively; and processing experiences of death and loss. Participants described profound shifts in their perspectives on aging, care relationships, and their professional identities; changes that persisted throughout their pre-clinical training. To conclude, early and sustained engagement with older adults in their home environment through a structured service-learning project enhanced medical students' professional development, empathy, and attitudes toward aging. Incorporating similar programs into medical education curricula may provide substantial pedagogical benefits. Future research should assess long-term impacts on career choices and care quality.

TI Approaches for cybersecurity education: a South African military science
   perspective
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Cybersecurity; cybersecurity education; education; pedagogical tools;
   student profiles; practical cybersecurity exercises; Social Sciences;
   Education; Adult Education and Lifelong Learning; Work-based Learning;
   Philosophy of Education; Research Methods in Education
AB Cybersecurity threats have escalated in South Africa, highlighting vulnerabilities across government, industry, and national infrastructure. This paper is a position article that synthesises and critiques literature and institutional practices to propose optimal, actionable approaches for cybersecurity education in a South African military context. This paper aims to identify optimal approaches for cybersecurity education in South Africa, with focused recommendations for the Faculty of Military Science and other higher education institutions. A position paper methodology synthesises recent literature, policy documents, and the National Initiative for Cybersecurity Education (NICE) curricular framework, evaluating educational models, student profiles, industry engagement, and active learning tools relevant to the South African context. Effective cybersecurity education requires multidisciplinary curricula, integration of technical and social science perspectives, practical exercises (like CTF and cyber ranges), and strong collaboration with industry and government. Student profiles should emphasise both technical acumen and soft skills. South African needs are best addressed by mapping local competencies to international frameworks while contextualising to national security priorities. Curricula must be updated to reflect multidisciplinary competencies, provide practical training, align with international standards, and foster industry/government partnerships to build resilience and professional capacity in South Africa's cybersecurity workforce.

TI Micro-credential for Industry 4.0: engineering safety and reliability
   with integrated machine learning and soft skills
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Micro-credential; engineering; machine learning; soft skills; lifelong
   learning; Micro-credential; engineering; machine learning; soft skills;
   lifelong learning
ID EDUCATION
AB The Fourth Industrial Revolution [Industry 4.0 (IR4.0)] transforms industries with intelligent machines, automation and data-driven decision-making. This paradigm shift demands a workforce equipped with both advanced technical know-how and strong soft skills. Traditional curriculum struggles to keep up, creating a skills gap. Targeted micro-credentials can close this gap by offering flexible, bite-sized training aligned to real-world needs. Unlike existing micro-credentials that focus on either hard or soft skills in isolation, this work presents Boosting Engineering Safety and Reliability for IR 4.0: Integrated Approach of Soft and Hard Skills (BESTARI 4.0), as a practical framework that links the macro-drivers of the IR4.0 competency requirements, such as cyber-physical automation, pervasive data and human-machine collaboration. It translates those requirements into micro-credential modules that weave together resource-constrained tiny machine learning-based engineering techniques with essential soft-skill development, such as critical thinking, communication and team problem-solving. The BESTARI 4.0 micro-credential, delivered on an open online platform, specifies clear learning objectives and experiential tasks, such as video labs, annotated notes, formative quizzes and real-world safety case studies. The ultimate outcome is the development of a proficient workforce in deploying machine learning solutions that enhance productivity while simultaneously improving safety and reliability within next-generation industrial settings.

TI School leaders' experiences of implementing and organizing full-day
   schooling as a school development project
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE School development; full-day schooling; school leadership; pedagogical
   practices; organizational change; Arts & Humanities; Humanities; Media &
   Film Studies; Journalism & Professional Media; Publishing; Arts; Music;
   Music & Education; Early Years; Social Sciences; Education
AB Full-day schooling, in this study, refers to an extended school day for pupils aged 6-9, including instruction, school meals, structured activities, and homework assistance. The article explores school leaders' experiences of implementing full-day schooling, highlighting practical and organizational impacts on teachers' work and pupils' learning. It investigates how school leaders experience the implementation of full-day schooling as part of a broader school development initiative, offering insights into the pedagogical and structural implications of such reform. The study is designed as a qualitative study using focus group interviews to collect data. The research was conducted within the context of a Norwegian pilot program involving a selected primary school and contributes to current discussions about educational reform and leadership in extended schooling models. The findings are relevant to policy makers, researchers and educational leaders aiming to improve school policy and development. The research emphasizes aligning organizational change with pedagogical practice to support diverse, practice-based learning. While the findings are primarily drawn from school leaders' own perspectives, the study raises critical questions about whether extended schooling alone can improve learning outcomes and reduce social inequalities. The study analyses how organizational and pedagogical aspects of educational reforms impact schools, offering a basis for improved future development strategies.

TI Beauriful harts or beautiful hearts: linguistic pattern analysis of
   misspellings of Arab EFL writers
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE English spelling system; error analysis; L1 transfer; misspelling
   patterns; morphological; phonological knowledge; English spelling
   system; error analysis; L1 transfer; misspelling patterns;
   morphological; ortho-graphic; phonological knowledge
ID PHONOLOGICAL AWARENESS; KNOWLEDGE; CHILDREN; ENGLISH; ERRORS; 1ST
AB In the era of spell-checks and autocorrection, the prescriptive question of a child's ability to spell has started to fizzle out, and many schools began to call off formal spelling instruction. This ideological and educational reform has resulted in changing spelling patterns and features. Informed by the triple-word-form theory of spelling development, this mixed-methods study analyzed English misspellings by Arab writers using the Phonological, Orthographic, and Morphological Analysis System (POMAS), a framework that has not been previously applied in this context. Ninety-four academic essays were examined, with misspellings first categorized-phonological, orthographic, or morphological-and then analyzed for linguistic patterns (e.g. epenthesis, syncope, voicing, etc.). The frequency analysis showed that orthographic errors (e.g. whith/with) were predominant (making up 68% of total errors) and mostly occurred in high-frequency words in General American English. The deep pattern analysis, however, showed (1) reduction of vowel digraphs, (2) omission of short vowels, and (3) fluctuations in silent letters, voiced-voiceless pairs, and ambiguous letters-features either absent or only partially present in learners' L1 (diglottic Arabic). We discuss these findings in relation to participants' L1 background and state of spelling ability, followed by some educational implications.

TI Generative AI adoption among arts and design students in Palestinian
   higher education: impacts on task completion
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Generative artificial intelligence; arts education; design pedagogy;
   task completion; Palestinian higher education; discipline-specific AI
   adoption
AB This study investigates Palestinian undergraduate arts and design students' attitudes towards generative artificial intelligence (GenAI) and examines the factors influencing their willingness to adopt these technologies in learning contexts. Data were collected from undergraduate students using an online survey and analysed through descriptive and inferential statistics, including chi-squared tests and regression analyses. The findings indicate that while students generally view the use of GenAI for academic tasks positively, its adoption is hindered by ethical, infrastructural, and cultural concerns. Notably, the results show a strong direct relationship between GenAI usage and perceived benefits (beta = 0.66) and a moderate link between usage and challenges (beta = 0.33). Although challenges weakly but significantly predict benefits (beta = 0.12), a mediation analysis suggests that overcoming these challenges can facilitate additional gains (beta = 0.04). Moderation effects reveal that more advanced students perceive challenges as less beneficial (beta =-0.12) but experience an enhanced effect of usage on perceived benefits (beta = 0.21). These outcomes underscore contradictions between students' enthusiasm for GenAI and their actual adoption of such tools, highlighting the need for robust policy guidelines, educator training, and supportive infrastructure. Addressing ethical concerns, promoting AI literacy, and ensuring equitable technology access emerge as pivotal strategies for maximising GenAI's transformative potential. Future research should investigate the long-term influence of GenAI on learning outcomes and further refine frameworks for its responsible and effective implementation in higher education.

TI Exploring the challenges Muslim female refugee students face in the
   educational context of high school in the United Kingdom
SO COGENT EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Muslim; refugee; girls; challenges; solutions; UK; Social Sciences;
   Education; Education Policy; Technology; Pedagogy
ID PERSPECTIVES; TEACHERS
AB This study aimed to explore the challenges Muslim female refugee students face in high school settings in the UK and the strategies they use to address these challenges. To achieve this, a qualitative research design using semi-structured interviews was employed to collect data from 10 Muslim female high school refugee students, selected through purposive sampling. The data were then analyzed using rigorous thematic analysis. The findings revealed that these girls grapple with challenges, including social ostracism, multifaceted discrimination, targeted bullying, and curricular sensitivity issues. To address these obstacles, some potential solutions were also included in the research. The study's findings could guide the formulation of policies and practices to facilitate the integration and empowerment of Muslim female refugee students, enabling them to achieve equitable participation in the United Kingdom's education system.

TI Little scientists, big impact: a STEM camp for endangered species
   awareness
SO SCIENCE ACTIVITIES-PROJECTS AND CURRICULUM IDEAS IN STEM CLASSROOMS
LA English
DT Article
DE Early childhood; environmental; climate change; ecology
AB Global climate change is a threat to biodiversity. Sustainability education and actions are essential to help with biodiversity conservation, yet there are gaps in understanding young children's ability to take action. This article presents several engaging activities for young children related to endangered species at risk due to climate change. TI Freeze for future: insights into co-creating a virtual learning
   environment with young people for climate change education
SO SCIENCE ACTIVITIES-PROJECTS AND CURRICULUM IDEAS IN STEM CLASSROOMS
LA English
DT Article
DE Alpine landscapes; citizen science; climate change education;
   transdisciplinarity; virtual reality
AB The consequences of anthropogenic climate change threaten the well-being of natural environments and lead to permanent changes in ecosystems. In high Alpine regions, glaciers serve as critical climate change indicators. Their presence makes them essential to climate change education. However, the rapid retreat of glaciers increases difficulties for school field trips to these areas. The citizen science project Freeze For Future - Young People create Virtual Glacier Worlds for the Future of Climate Change Education thus aimed at developing a virtual glacier field trip in collaboration with 380 high school students and three researchers from the University of Innsbruck (Austria) in order to create an innovative application for future educational purposes. The final version of the VR environment includes a virtual field trip following the young people's tracks on seven stations through the Gaisberg Valley, including experiences beyond reality, such as time leaps. This study provides insights into the projects' research design, the implementation as well as cooperation with stakeholders in and outside academia.

AF Rosas, Richard E.
   Slowey, Niall C.
TI Teaching environmental change through coral isotopes
SO SCIENCE ACTIVITIES-PROJECTS AND CURRICULUM IDEAS IN STEM CLASSROOMS
LA English
DT Article
DE Collegiate environmental science; chemistry; corals; isotopes;
   inquiry-based learning
ID SEA-SURFACE TEMPERATURE; SCALE ATMOSPHERIC CIRCULATION; OCEAN
   ACIDIFICATION; CLIMATE-CHANGE; MARINE ORGANISMS; VARIABILITY; IMPACTS;
   RECONSTRUCTION; DISTRIBUTIONS; ANOMALIES
AB Climate and other environmental changes, especially seawater temperature and pH, are of interest to science disciplines. This article presents a two-part activity that introduces high school or college students to geological/paleoclimate techniques used to reconstruct past temperatures and pH from the isotopic compositions of marine coral skeletons. Students will learn about environmental changes from natural climate cycles and anthropogenic forcing: Part 1 considers the greenhouse effect and variations in seawater temperature, and Part 2 considers variations in seawater pH due to natural seasonal and anthropogenic forcing. Each part can be used individually or as a series (whichever meets instructors' needs). Since active learning strategies directed by guided inquiry have great efficacy, this activity pairs selected readings to establish prior knowledge with comprehension questions, and it implements a Microsoft Excel exercise that allows students to analyze a tangible example of real-world coral isotope data and draw conclusions from created graphs. Thus, students will learn about the use of paleoclimate proxies and apply this knowledge by calculating seawater temperature and pH values from coral skeletal isotopes values. Students gain a transdisciplinary view of the biological and chemical interactions of seawater and corals aligning with learning objectives for aquatic and environmental science courses.
